####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\genai\prompt\__init__.py
####################################################################################################

"""
Prompt management package for the YONO Business GenAI module.

Provides the :class:`PromptManager` for loading and rendering Jinja2
YAML prompt templates, and re-exports the prompt file paths as
constants for convenience.

Public API
----------
>>> from dlh_ai.genai.prompt import PromptManager
>>> manager = PromptManager()
>>> system_prompt = manager.get_prompt("yono_business_chatbot", "system")
"""

from ._manager import PromptManager

__all__ = ["PromptManager"]



####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\genai\prompt\_manager.py
####################################################################################################


"""
Prompt template manager for the YONO Business RAG chatbot.

Loads all YAML prompt files eagerly at construction time and renders them
via Jinja2.  Thread-safe: the rendered string is built from immutable
template objects and the read-only registry.
"""

import logging
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any

import yaml
from jinja2 import Environment, StrictUndefined, Undefined

logger = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")


@dataclass
class PromptManager:
    """Manages loading and rendering prompts from YAML files in the prompt directory.

    This class provides methods to get prompts by agent name and type, and to render
    them with context variables. It supports strict template rendering and caching of
    loaded prompts.

    Attributes:
    ----------
    strict_templates : bool
        Whether to enforce strict template rendering. Defaults to True.
    
    Examples:
    --------
    >>> manager = PromptManager()
    >>> prompt = manager.get_prompt("agent_name", "instructions")
    """

    strict_templates: bool = True

    # Private attributes, not part of the public dataclass interface.
    _prompt_dir: Path = field(init=False, repr=False)
    _prompt_registry: dict[str, dict[str, str]] = field(
        init=False, default_factory=dict, repr=False
    )
    _jinja_env: Environment = field(init=False, repr=False)

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        self._prompt_dir = Path(__file__).parent
        self._prompt_registry = {}
        self._jinja_env = Environment(
            undefined=StrictUndefined if self.strict_templates else Undefined,
            autoescape=False,          # Prompts are plain text, not HTML.
            keep_trailing_newline=True,
        )
        self._load_all_prompts()

    def _load_all_prompts(self) -> None:
        """Eagerly load all YAML prompt files from the prompt directory."""
        self._prompt_registry.clear()

        for file_path in self._prompt_dir.glob("*.yaml"):
            agent_name = file_path.stem
            try:
                with file_path.open("r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
                    prompts = yaml.safe_load(f) or {}

                if not isinstance(prompts, dict):
                    logger.error(
                        "Prompt file for agent '%s' must contain a YAML mapping "
                        "at root. Got: %s",
                        agent_name,
                        type(prompts).__name__,
                    )
                    prompts = {}

                self._prompt_registry[agent_name] = prompts
            except Exception:
                logger.exception(
                    "Error loading prompts for agent '%s' from '%s'.",
                    agent_name,
                    file_path,
                )
                self._prompt_registry[agent_name] = {}

    def get_prompt(self, agent_name: str, prompt_key: str, **kwargs: Any) -> str:
        """Retrieves a prompt and renders it with the provided keyword arguments.

        Parameters:
        -----------
           agent_name (str): The name of the agent. This must match the filename of the YAML file in the prompt directory.
           prompt_key (str): The key of the prompt within the agent's YAML file.
           **kwargs (Any): Keyword arguments to pass to the Jinja2 template which will replace variable with actual value during runtime.
           
        Returns:
        --------
           str: The rendered prompt string.

        Raises:
           ValueError: If the prompt is not found or the template rendering fails due missing template variables.

        Example:
        --------
            >>> pm = PromptManager()
            >>> prompt = pm.get_prompt("agent1", "system", variable="value")
            >>> prompt2 = pm.get_prompt("my_awesome_agent", "instruction", column_name=["col1", "col2", "col3"])

        Notes:
        ------
            - The `agent_name` and `prompt_key` are used to locate the prompt in the registry.
            - The `**kwargs` are used to render the Jinja2 template.
        """
        prompts = self._prompt_registry.get(agent_name, {})
        template_str = prompts.get(prompt_key, "")
        if not isinstance(template_str, str) or not template_str:
            raise ValueError(f"Prompt not found: {agent_name}.{prompt_key}")

        template = self._jinja_env.from_string(template_str)
        return template.render(**kwargs)

    def clear_cache(self, agent_name: str | None = None) -> None:
        if agent_name is None:
            self._prompt_registry.clear()
        else:
            self._prompt_registry.pop(agent_name, None)

    def reload(self) -> None:
        """Reload all prompt YAML files from disk."""
        self._load_all_prompts()

# Guardrail Rejection Messages -------------------------------------------
# Distinct, user-facing reply messages shown to the customer whenever the
# GuardrailService (see rag/_guardrails.py) or the RAG pipeline blocks a
# request. Each key corresponds 1:1 to a domain exception scenario, so the
# customer gets an actionable, scenario-specific message instead of one
# generic "something went wrong" reply.
#
# These are plain, static messages (no Jinja variables) rendered directly
# via PromptManager.get_prompt("guardrail_messages", "<key>") from the global
# exception handler in application/backend/main.py - they are NOT sent to
# the LLM, they are the final text returned to the user.
#
# Key -> exception class mapping (see rag/exceptions.py):
#   clarification_needed -> ClarificationNeededError
#   unsupported_language -> UnsupportedLanguageError
#   profanity         -> ProfanityDetectedError
#   toxicity          -> ToxicContentError
#   pii_detected      -> PIIDetectedError
#   prompt_injection  -> PromptInjectionDetectedError
#   out_of_domain     -> OutOfDomainError
#   hallucination     -> HallucinationDetectedError
#   invalid_citation  -> InvalidCitationError
#   generic           -> GuardrailViolationError (base class, no more
#                        specific subclass matched)
#   service_unavailable -> any other YonoRagError (non-guardrail failure,
#                        e.g. Elasticsearch/LLM/embedding errors)

clarification_needed: |
  Could you share a few more details about what you'd like help with?
  For example, mention the specific service - such as login, fund
  transfer, cards, or FD/RD - so I can guide you accurately.

unsupported_language: |
  I currently support questions only in English. Please type your
  question in English and I'll be happy to assist. For help in other
  languages, contact the YONO helpline at 1800-1234 (toll-free, 24x7).

profanity: |
  I'm here to help with your YONO banking questions, but I'm unable to
  continue if the language used is inappropriate. Please rephrase your
  question politely and I'll be happy to assist. For immediate help, call
  the YONO helpline at 1800-1234 (toll-free, 24x7).

toxicity: |
  I'm unable to process that message as it was flagged as hostile or
  harmful content. Please rephrase your question in a respectful manner
  and I'll be glad to help with your YONO banking query. For urgent
  assistance, contact the YONO helpline at 1800-1234 (toll-free, 24x7).

pii_detected: |
  For your security, I can't process messages that include sensitive
  personal or financial details such as your PAN, Aadhaar, card, or
  account number. Please resend your question without this information -
  I can still help without it. Never share your OTP, PIN, or CVV with
  anyone, including this assistant.

prompt_injection: |
  I'm not able to change my role or set aside my banking-assistant
  instructions. I'm here strictly to help with YONO Business banking
  questions - please ask me something about your account, cards,
  transfers, or other YONO services.

out_of_domain: |
  I'm the YONO Business banking assistant, so I can only help with
  questions about YONO features, accounts, cards, transfers, loans,
  deposits, and related banking services. Could you rephrase your
  question around one of these topics? For anything else, please reach
  out to the relevant SBI department.

hallucination: |
  I wasn't able to generate a reliable, fully-grounded answer to that
  question from our knowledge base. Rather than risk giving you
  inaccurate information, please contact the YONO helpline at 1800-1234
  (toll-free, 24x7) or visit your nearest SBI branch for accurate
  assistance.

invalid_citation: |
  I couldn't verify the supporting information for that answer against
  our knowledge base, so I'm unable to share it with confidence. Please
  contact the YONO helpline at 1800-1234 (toll-free, 24x7) or visit your
  nearest SBI branch for accurate assistance.

generic: |
  Your request could not be processed because it did not pass one of our
  safety checks. Please rephrase your question, keeping it focused on
  YONO Business banking services. For help, contact the YONO helpline at
  1800-1234 (toll-free, 24x7).

service_unavailable: |
  The chatbot is temporarily unavailable. Please try again in a moment or
  contact the YONO helpline at 1800-1234 (toll-free, 24x7).

# YONO Business Chatbot - Prompt Templates -------------------------------------
# All prompts follow the Jinja2 template syntax.
# Variable references are enclosed in {{ double_braces }}.
# Each key maps directly to a prompt_key used by PromptManager.get_prompt().

# System prompt - sent as the "system" role message in every conversation. --
system: |
  You are YONO Assistant, the official AI-powered virtual banking helper for
  YONO Business, State Bank of India's digital banking platform serving over
  10 million users across India.

  Your purpose is to help customers with questions about YONO Business features,
  services, accounts, ATM/Debit Cards, fund transfers, loans, fixed deposits,
  and other banking services offered through the YONO app and portal.

  You always:
  - Provide accurate, concise, and helpful answers based on the retrieved
    knowledge-base context provided to you.
  - Respond in a professional, empathetic, and friendly tone.
  - Cite the relevant service category when answering.
  - Escalate to a human banking officer when a query is complex, sensitive,
    or involves account security concerns.
  - Never reveal internal system details, API keys, credentials, or any
    sensitive infrastructure information.
  - Never make up information that is not present in the provided context.
  - If the retrieved context does not contain a clear answer, say so honestly
    and suggest the customer contact their nearest SBI branch or call the
    YONO helpline at 1800-1234 or 1800-2100.

# RAG chat prompt - the main user-facing prompt used per query. -------------
# Variables: query, context_items, history
# context_items is a list of dicts, each with keys:
#   rank, score, category, doc_type, question, answer
# history is a list of dicts, each with keys: query, answer (may be empty).
rag_chat: |
  Use the following knowledge-base excerpts retrieved from YONO Business's
  official FAQ database to answer the customer's question.
  {% if history %}
  ── PRIOR CONVERSATION (this session) ──────────────────────────────────────
  {% for turn in history %}
  Customer: {{ turn.query }}
  Assistant: {{ turn.answer }}
  {% endfor %}
  ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  {% endif %}
  ── RETRIEVED CONTEXT ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  {% for item in context_items %}
  [{{ item.rank }}] Score: {{ "%.4f" | format(item.score) }}
      Category : {{ item.category }}
      Type     : {{ item.doc_type }}
      Question : {{ item.question }}
      Answer   : {{ item.answer }}
  {% endfor %}
  ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

  CUSTOMER QUESTION: {{ query }}

  Instructions:
  1. Answer the customer's question directly and concisely using the context.
  2. If multiple excerpts are relevant, synthesise them into a single answer.
  3. If the context does not contain sufficient information, say:
     "I'm sorry, I couldn't find a specific answer for that. Please contact
      the YONO helpline at 1800-1234 or visit your nearest SBI branch."
  4. Do NOT mention internal scores, ranks, or system details.
  5. Always end with: "Is there anything else I can help you with today?"

  ANSWER:

# Fallback prompt - used when no relevant context is retrieved. -------------
# Variables: query
fallback: |
  The customer asked: {{ query }}

  Unfortunately, the knowledge base does not contain a specific answer to
  this question. Provide a polite, empathetic response that:
  1. Acknowledges the customer's query.
  2. Apologises for the inability to provide a direct answer.
  3. Directs the customer to:
     - YONO Helpline: 1800-1234 / 1800-2100 (toll-free, 24×7)
     - Nearest SBI Branch
     - SBI official website: https://www.onlinesbi.sbi
  4. Ends by asking if there is anything else you can help with.

# Greeting prompt - used when the user sends a greeting. -------------------
# Variables: (none)
greeting: |
  Welcome to YONO Business Assistant!

  I am your virtual banking assistant for YONO, State Bank of India's
  all-in-one digital banking platform.

  I can help you with:
  • ATM / Debit Card services
  • Fund transfers and payments
  • Account balance and transaction enquiries
  • Fixed Deposits and Recurring Deposits
  • Loan account details
  • YONO app features and navigation
  • And much more!

  Please type your question and I will do my best to assist you.

# Error prompt - shown to the user when a backend error occurs. ------------
# Variables: (none)
error: |
  I apologise. I encountered a technical issue while processing your request.

  Please try again in a moment. If the issue persists, contact the YONO
  helpline at 1800-1234 (toll-free, available 24×7) for immediate assistance.

  We are sorry for the inconvenience.

"""
RAG (Retrieval-Augmented Generation) package for the YONO Business Chatbot.

This package contains the complete enterprise-grade RAG pipeline:

* ``exceptions`` - Domain-specific exception hierarchy.
* ``_models`` - Pydantic data-transfer objects for all pipeline boundaries.
* ``_es_client`` - Elasticsearch client factory with health verification.
* ``_embedding_service`` - SentenceTransformer model service loaded from HDFS.
* ``_ingestion`` - Knowledge-base CSV ingestion pipeline with bulk indexing.
* ``_retrieval`` - kNN / hybrid vector search and chat response assembly.
* ``_reranker`` - Optional ONNX/torch cross-encoder reranking of retrieved candidates.
* ``_chat_history`` - Optional Elasticsearch-backed multi-turn chat history, plus
  server-side session-ID generation.

####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\genai\rag\_chat_history.py
####################################################################################################

"""
Server-side session IDs and Elasticsearch-backed multi-turn chat history.

Provides:
* :func:`generate_session_id` - a cryptographically random 12-digit session
  identifier, generated whenever an incoming request has none.
* :class:`ChatHistoryService` - an optional, disabled-by-default service that
  persists each (query, answer) turn to a dedicated Elasticsearch index and
  reloads prior turns for a session so they can be fed back into the LLM
  prompt as conversational memory.

Session-ID generation and history persistence are intentionally decoupled:
every request gets a session ID regardless of whether persistence is turned
on, but history is only loaded/saved when :attr:`ChatHistoryService.enabled`
is ``True`` and a real Elasticsearch client has been configured.
"""

import logging
import secrets
import threading
import time
from dataclasses import dataclass, field

from elasticsearch import Elasticsearch

LOGGER = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")

_SESSION_ID_DIGITS = 12
_DIGITS = "0123456789"


def generate_session_id() -> str:
    """Generate a cryptographically random 12-digit numeric session ID.

    Returns
    -------
    str
        A 12-character string of ASCII digits, e.g. ``"048213975610"``.
        Leading zeros are allowed, this is an opaque identifier, not a number.

    Examples
    --------
    >>> session_id = generate_session_id()
    >>> len(session_id)
    12
    >>> session_id.isdigit()
    True
    """
    return "".join(secrets.choice(_DIGITS) for _ in range(_SESSION_ID_DIGITS))


@dataclass
class ChatHistoryService:
    """Optional Elasticsearch-backed store for multi-turn chat history.

    Disabled by default: when :attr:`enabled` is ``False`` (or no
    Elasticsearch :attr:`client` has been configured), every method is a
    zero-cost no-op, no Elasticsearch calls are ever made. This mirrors the
    "optional service, disabled until infra is staged" pattern already used
    by :class:`~dlh_ai.genai.rag.RerankerService`.

    Per-request failures in :meth:`load_history` / :meth:`save_turn` are
    caught and logged, never raised, so an outage in this secondary store
    never breaks a customer-facing chat answer.

    Parameters
    ----------
    enabled : bool
        Master toggle for multi-turn history. Default is ``False``.
    client : Elasticsearch, optional
        Elasticsearch client for the *dedicated* chat-history cluster/index
        (independent of the ``[rag]`` knowledge-base client). ``None`` when
        disabled.
    index_name : str
        Elasticsearch index that stores chat turns.
    max_history_turns : int
        Maximum number of prior turns loaded per session and injected into
        the LLM prompt. Default is 5.

    Examples
    --------
    >>> service = ChatHistoryService(enabled=False)
    >>> service.load_history("048213975610")
    []
    """

    enabled: bool = False
    client: Elasticsearch | None = None
    index_name: str = ""
    max_history_turns: int = 5

    _lock: threading.Lock = field(init=False, repr=False)
    _sequence: int = field(init=False, repr=False, default=0)

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        self._lock = threading.Lock()
        self._sequence = 0
        if self.enabled and self.client is not None:
            self._ensure_index()

    def _ensure_index(self) -> None:
        """Create the chat-history index if it does not already exist.

        Failures are logged, not raised, since a pre-existing index (or a
        transient issue that resolves before the first real request) should
        not prevent application startup.
        """
        try:
            if not self.client.indices.exists(index=self.index_name):
                self.client.indices.create(
                    index=self.index_name,
                    mappings={
                        "properties": {
                            "session_id": {"type": "keyword"},
                            "query": {"type": "text"},
                            "answer": {"type": "text"},
                            "timestamp": {"type": "date", "format": "epoch_millis"},
                            "seq": {"type": "long"},
                        }
                    },
                )
                LOGGER.info("Created chat-history index '%s'.", self.index_name)
        except Exception:
            LOGGER.exception(
                "Failed to ensure chat-history index '%s' exists.", self.index_name
            )

    def load_history(self, session_id: str) -> list[dict[str, str]]:
        """Load prior turns for a session, oldest first.

        Parameters
        ----------
        session_id : str
            Session identifier to load turns for.

        Returns
        -------
        list[dict[str, str]]
            Up to :attr:`max_history_turns` prior turns, each
            ``{"query": ..., "answer": ...}``, in chronological order.
            Returns ``[]`` when disabled, no client is configured, or the
            lookup fails for any reason.

        Examples
        --------
        >>> service.load_history("048213975610")
        [{'query': 'How do I block my card?', 'answer': 'Use the YONO app...'}]
        """
        if not self.enabled or self.client is None or not session_id:
            return []

        try:
            with self._lock:
                response = self.client.search(
                    index=self.index_name,
                    query={"term": {"session_id": session_id}},
                    sort=[{"timestamp": "desc"}, {"seq": "desc"}],
                    size=self.max_history_turns,
                )
            hits = response.get("hits", {}).get("hits", [])
            sources = [hit.get("_source", {}) for hit in hits]
            # Re-sort ascending by (timestamp, seq) rather than trusting hit
            # order to be exactly reversed: multiple turns saved within the
            # same millisecond would otherwise land in the wrong order.
            sources.sort(key=lambda src: (src.get("timestamp", 0), src.get("seq", 0)))
            return [
                {
                    "query": str(source.get("query", "")),
                    "answer": str(source.get("answer", "")),
                }
                for source in sources
            ]
        except Exception:
            LOGGER.exception(
                "Failed to load chat history for session '%s'.", session_id
            )
            return []

    def save_turn(self, session_id: str, query: str, answer: str) -> None:
        """Persist a single (query, answer) turn for a session.

        Parameters
        ----------
        session_id : str
            Session identifier the turn belongs to.
        query : str
            The user's question.
        answer : str
            The final (guardrail-validated) answer shown to the user.

        Notes
        -----
        No-ops when disabled, no client is configured, or ``session_id`` is
        empty. Any Elasticsearch failure is logged and swallowed, never
        raised, so a chat-history outage never breaks a chat response.

        Examples
        --------
        >>> service.save_turn("048213975610", "How do I block my card?", "...")
        """
        if not self.enabled or self.client is None or not session_id:
            return

        try:
            with self._lock:
                self._sequence += 1
                self.client.index(
                    index=self.index_name,
                    document={
                        "session_id": session_id,
                        "query": query,
                        "answer": answer,
                        "timestamp": int(time.time() * 1000),
                        "seq": self._sequence,
                    },
                )
        except Exception:
            LOGGER.exception(
                "Failed to save chat turn for session '%s'.", session_id
            )



####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\genai\rag\_embedding_service.py
####################################################################################################

"""
Embedding service for the YONO Business RAG pipeline.

GPU-accelerated, thread-safe wrapper around the ``sentence-transformers``
``SentenceTransformer`` model loaded from HDFS.

Optimisation highlights (thousands of concurrent users, NVIDIA GPU):
* **CUDA auto-detection**: moves the model to the first available GPU;
  falls back to CPU gracefully.
* **FP16 / half-precision**: halves GPU VRAM usage and speeds up
  inference on Tensor-Core GPUs (Volta+).
* **Dynamic batching**: encode_query uses a background ``BatchProcessor``
  that accumulates single-query requests from concurrent request handlers
  into GPU-sized batches, then dispatches them together; reduces kernel
  launch overhead by ~10x at high QPS.
* **Thread-safety**: a single ``threading.Lock`` guards the synchronous
  model.encode() call so concurrent threads don't cause CUDA context races.
* The service is stateless between calls; safe to share across all asyncio
  tasks / Uvicorn worker threads.
"""

import logging
import threading
from concurrent.futures import Future
from dataclasses import dataclass, field

import numpy as np
import torch
from sentence_transformers import SentenceTransformer

from ...utils import HDFSClient
from .exceptions import (
    EmbeddingDimensionMismatchError,
    EmbeddingEncodingError,
    EmbeddingModelError,
)

LOGGER = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")

# How long (seconds) the batch collector waits before flushing a partial batch.
_BATCH_TIMEOUT_S: float = 0.005  # 5 ms


@dataclass
class EmbeddingService:
    """GPU-accelerated SentenceTransformer embedding service.

    The model is loaded from HDFS once during ``__post_init__`` and
    reused for all subsequent encode calls.  At high concurrency the
    :meth:`encode_query` hot-path uses a micro-batching collector so
    individual per-request encode() calls are automatically coalesced
    into larger GPU batches for maximum throughput.

    Parameters
    ----------
    hdfs_model_path : str
        Absolute HDFS path to the SentenceTransformer snapshot directory.
    expected_dimension : int
        The embedding dimension that this service must produce.
        Must match the ``dims`` value in the Elasticsearch index mapping.
        Default is 384 (all-MiniLM-L12-v2).
    use_fp16 : bool
        Cast the model to FP16 on CUDA devices to halve VRAM usage and
        improve throughput on Tensor-Core GPUs.  Default is ``True``.
    gpu_batch_size : int
        Number of texts per GPU forward pass inside :meth:`encode`.
        Tune to fill GPU VRAM without OOM. Default is 256.
    dynamic_batching : bool
        Enable micro-batch collector for :meth:`encode_query`.
        Recommended for high-QPS deployments. Default is ``True``.
    dynamic_batch_max_size : int
        Maximum queries to collect before a forced GPU dispatch.
        Default is 64.

    Attributes
    ----------
    model : SentenceTransformer
        Loaded model instance. Populated during ``__post_init__``.
    device : str
        Torch device string, e.g. ``"cuda:0"`` or ``"cpu"``.

    Raises
    ------
    EmbeddingModelError
        If the model cannot be loaded from HDFS.
    EmbeddingDimensionMismatchError
        If the loaded model's output dimension does not match
        ``expected_dimension``.
    """

    hdfs_model_path: str
    expected_dimension: int = 384
    use_fp16: bool = True
    gpu_batch_size: int = 256
    dynamic_batching: bool = True
    dynamic_batch_max_size: int = 64

    # Private, populated by __post_init__
    model: SentenceTransformer = field(init=False)
    device: str = field(init=False)
    _lock: threading.Lock = field(init=False, default_factory=threading.Lock, repr=False)

    # Dynamic-batching collector state
    _batch_thread: threading.Thread | None = field(init=False, default=None, repr=False)
    _batch_queue: list[tuple] = field(init=False, default_factory=list, repr=False)
    _batch_lock: threading.Lock = field(init=False, default_factory=threading.Lock, repr=False)
    _batch_event: threading.Event = field(init=False, default_factory=threading.Event, repr=False)
    _stop_event: threading.Event = field(init=False, default_factory=threading.Event, repr=False)

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        self.device = self._resolve_device()
        self.model = self._load_model()
        if self.dynamic_batching:
            self._start_batch_worker()

    # Public interface --------------------------------------------------

    def encode(
        self,
        texts: list[str],
        batch_size: int | None = None,
        normalize_embeddings: bool = True,
    ) -> np.ndarray:
        """Encode a list of texts into normalised embedding vectors.

        Thread-safe; uses an internal lock so concurrent threads share one
        GPU context without CUDA race conditions.

        Parameters
        ----------
        texts : list[str]
            Non-empty list of strings to encode.
        batch_size : int or None, optional
            Override the default ``gpu_batch_size``.  Pass ``None`` to use
            the service default.
        normalize_embeddings : bool, optional
            L2-normalise output so cosine similarity == dot product.
            Default is ``True``.

        Returns
        -------
        numpy.ndarray
            Float32 array of shape ``(len(texts), expected_dimension)``.

        Raises
        ------
        ValueError
            If ``texts`` is empty.
        EmbeddingEncodingError
            If the model's ``encode`` method raises any exception.
        """
        if not texts:
            raise ValueError(
                "EmbeddingService.encode() received an empty text list. "
                "Provide at least one text to encode."
            )

        effective_batch = batch_size or self.gpu_batch_size

        try:
            with self._lock:
                vectors: np.ndarray = self.model.encode(
                    texts,
                    batch_size=effective_batch,
                    normalize_embeddings=normalize_embeddings,
                    show_progress_bar=False,
                    convert_to_numpy=True,
                    device=self.device,
                )
        except Exception as exc:
            raise EmbeddingEncodingError(
                f"Failed to encode a batch of {len(texts)} texts: "
                f"{type(exc).__name__} - {exc}"
            ) from exc

        return vectors.astype(np.float32)

    def encode_query(self, query: str) -> list[float]:
        """Encode a single query string into a flat float list.

        At high concurrency, when ``dynamic_batching=True``, requests are
        automatically coalesced in the background batch worker for better
        GPU utilisation.  Callers block only until their specific result
        is ready (typically < 10 ms at 1000 QPS).

        Parameters
        ----------
        query : str
            The user's natural-language query.

        Returns
        -------
        list[float]
            Flat list of ``expected_dimension`` floats for Elasticsearch kNN.

        Raises
        ------
        ValueError
            If ``query`` is empty.
        EmbeddingEncodingError
            If encoding fails.
        """
        if not query or not query.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "EmbeddingService.encode_query() received an empty query string."
            )

        clean_query = query.strip()

        if self.dynamic_batching:
            return self._encode_via_batch_worker(clean_query)

        vectors = self.encode([clean_query])
        return vectors[0].tolist()

    def shutdown(self) -> None:
        """Stop the background batch worker gracefully.

        Call this on application shutdown (e.g. from FastAPI lifespan).
        """
        if self._stop_event is not None:
            self._stop_event.set()
            self._batch_event.set()  # Wake the worker so it can exit
        if self._batch_thread is not None and self._batch_thread.is_alive():
            self._batch_thread.join(timeout=2.0)

    # Private: device detection and model loading ------------------------

    @staticmethod
    def _resolve_device() -> str:
        """Return the best available torch device string."""
        if torch.cuda.is_available():
            device = "cuda:0"
            gpu_name = torch.cuda.get_device_name(0)
            total_mem_gb = torch.cuda.get_device_properties(0).total_memory / 1e9
            LOGGER.info(
                "GPU detected: %s (%.1f GB VRAM), embedding model will run on %s.",
                gpu_name,
                total_mem_gb,
                device,
            )
        else:
            device = "cpu"
            LOGGER.warning(
                "No CUDA GPU detected, embedding model will run on CPU. "
                "Performance will be significantly lower at high concurrency."
            )
        return device

    def _load_model(self) -> SentenceTransformer:
        """Download from HDFS and load the SentenceTransformer model onto GPU."""
        LOGGER.info("Initialising HDFS client for model download...")
        hdfs_client = HDFSClient()

        LOGGER.info("Loading embedding model from HDFS: %s", self.hdfs_model_path)
        try:
            model: SentenceTransformer = hdfs_client.load_model_sentence_transformer(
                self.hdfs_model_path,
                device=self.device,
            )
        except Exception as exc:
            raise EmbeddingModelError(
                f"Failed to load SentenceTransformer model from HDFS path "
                f"'{self.hdfs_model_path}': {type(exc).__name__} - {exc}"
            ) from exc

        # Validate embedding dimension.
        dimension: int | None = model.get_sentence_embedding_dimension()
        LOGGER.info("Embedding model loaded. Dimension: %s | Device: %s", dimension, self.device)

        if dimension != self.expected_dimension:
            raise EmbeddingDimensionMismatchError(
                f"Expected embedding dimension {self.expected_dimension}, "
                f"but the loaded model returned {dimension}. "
                f"Verify 'rag.embedding_dimension' in config.toml matches "
                f"the model at '{self.hdfs_model_path}'."
            )

        # Cast to FP16 on CUDA to cut VRAM in half and accelerate Tensor-Core ops.
        # Guarded: some model architectures/GPUs raise or produce unstable
        # (NaN) output under FP16, so fall back to FP32 rather than crash or
        # silently serve broken embeddings.
        if self.use_fp16 and self.device.startswith("cuda"):
            try:
                model = model.half()
                LOGGER.info("Embedding model cast to FP16 for GPU inference.")
            except RuntimeError as exc:
                LOGGER.warning(
                    "FP16 cast failed (%s); continuing with FP32 precision.",
                    exc,
                )

        LOGGER.info("Embedding dimension validated (%s). Service ready.", dimension)
        return model

    # Private: dynamic micro-batching -------------------------------------

    def _start_batch_worker(self) -> None:
        """Spawn the background thread that collects and dispatches batches."""
        self._stop_event = threading.Event()
        self._batch_thread = threading.Thread(
            target=self._batch_worker_loop,
            name="embedding-batch-worker",
            daemon=True,
        )
        self._batch_thread.start()
        LOGGER.info(
            "Dynamic batch worker started (max_size=%d, timeout=%.1f ms).",
            self.dynamic_batch_max_size,
            _BATCH_TIMEOUT_S * 1000,
        )

    def _batch_worker_loop(self) -> None:
        """Continuously drain the queue and dispatch GPU batches."""
        while not self._stop_event.is_set():
            # Wait for the first item or stop signal.
            self._batch_event.wait(timeout=_BATCH_TIMEOUT_S)
            self._batch_event.clear()

            with self._batch_lock:
                if not self._batch_queue:
                    continue
                # Grab up to dynamic_batch_max_size items.
                batch = self._batch_queue[: self.dynamic_batch_max_size]
                del self._batch_queue[: self.dynamic_batch_max_size]

            texts: list[str] = [item[0] for item in batch]
            futures: list[Future[list[float]]] = [item[1] for item in batch]

            try:
                vectors = self.encode(texts)
                for future, vec in zip(futures, vectors):
                    if not future.done():
                        try:
                            future.set_result(vec.tolist())
                        except Exception:
                            pass
            except Exception as exc:
                for future in futures:
                    if not future.done():
                        try:
                            future.set_exception(exc)
                        except Exception:
                            pass

    def _encode_via_batch_worker(self, query: str) -> list[float]:
        """Submit a single query to the batch worker and block until done."""
        from concurrent.futures import Future as CFFuture

        future: CFFuture[list[float]] = CFFuture()

        with self._batch_lock:
            self._batch_queue.append((query, future))
            # If we've accumulated a full batch, signal immediately.
            if len(self._batch_queue) >= self.dynamic_batch_max_size:
                self._batch_event.set()
            else:
                self._batch_event.set()  # Always wake; worker respects timeout

        try:
            return future.result(timeout=30.0)
        except Exception as exc:
            raise EmbeddingEncodingError(
                f"Dynamic batch encode failed for query: {type(exc).__name__} - {exc}"
            ) from exc



####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\genai\rag\_es_client.py
####################################################################################################

"""
Elasticsearch client factory for the YONO Business RAG pipeline.

This module is responsible for creating, configuring, and verifying
the Elasticsearch client used throughout the RAG pipeline. All
connection settings are sourced from the application configuration,
never hardcoded, so secrets remain out of source control.

The factory uses a connection pool with retry logic and configurable
timeouts to handle the scale of 10 million concurrent users.
"""

import logging

from elasticsearch import Elasticsearch, ConnectionError as ESConnectionError, TransportError

from .exceptions import ElasticsearchConnectionError, RagConfigurationError

LOGGER = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")


def build_es_client(
    host: str,
    api_key: str,
    request_timeout: int = 30,
    max_retries: int = 3,
    retry_on_timeout: bool = True,
    verify_certs: bool = True,
    connections_per_node: int = 10,
) -> Elasticsearch:
    """Create a configured Elasticsearch client instance.

    The client is constructed with a connection pool sized for high
    concurrency (``connections_per_node``), automatic retry on
    transient timeouts, and HTTPS support.

    Parameters
    ----------
    host : str
        Elasticsearch cluster URL including scheme and port,
        e.g. ``"https://host:9201"``.
    api_key : str
        API key for authenticating with Elasticsearch.
        Sourced from environment / ``.env``, never logged or printed.
    request_timeout : int, optional
        Per-request socket timeout in seconds. Default is 30.
    max_retries : int, optional
        Number of automatic retries on transient failures. Default is 3.
    retry_on_timeout : bool, optional
        Whether to retry the request if it times out. Default is True.
    verify_certs : bool, optional
        Whether to verify the TLS certificate. Default is True. Only set
        to False for a UAT environment with self-signed certificates,
        never in production.
    connections_per_node : int, optional
        Size of the connection pool per Elasticsearch node. Increase
        for high-concurrency deployments. Default is 10.

    Returns
    -------
    Elasticsearch
        Ready-to-use Elasticsearch client instance.

    Raises
    ------
    RagConfigurationError
        Raised when ``host`` or ``api_key`` is empty or ``None``.
    ElasticsearchConnectionError
        Raised when the cluster health check fails after client creation,
        indicating the cluster is unreachable or authentication failed.

    Notes
    -----
    * TLS certificate verification is enabled by default. Disable it only
      for a UAT environment with self-signed certificates by setting
      ``RAG__VERIFY_CERTS=false`` in the environment, never in production.
    * The connection pool size should match or exceed the number of
      asyncio worker tasks expected to hit Elasticsearch simultaneously.

    Examples
    --------
    >>> client = build_es_client(
    ...     host="https://es-host:9201",
    ...     api_key="<api_key_from_env>",
    ... )
    >>> info = client.info()
    >>> print(info["version"]["number"])
    """
    if not host:
        raise RagConfigurationError(
            "Elasticsearch 'host' must not be empty. "
            "Set 'rag.elasticsearch_host' in config.toml or "
            "'ELASTICSEARCH__HOST' in the environment."
        )
    if not api_key:
        raise RagConfigurationError(
            "Elasticsearch 'api_key' must not be empty. "
            "Set 'ELASTICSEARCH__API_KEY' in the .env file or environment."
        )

    LOGGER.info("Creating Elasticsearch client for host: %s", host)

    client = Elasticsearch(
        host,
        api_key=api_key,
        request_timeout=request_timeout,
        max_retries=max_retries,
        retry_on_timeout=retry_on_timeout,
        verify_certs=verify_certs,
        connections_per_node=connections_per_node,
    )

    _verify_connection(client, host)
    return client


def _verify_connection(client: Elasticsearch, host: str) -> None:
    """Verify the Elasticsearch connection by calling cluster info.

    Parameters
    ----------
    client : Elasticsearch
        The client instance to verify.
    host : str
        Host string used only for error message context.

    Raises
    ------
    ElasticsearchConnectionError
        Raised when ``client.info()`` raises a connection or transport error,
        meaning the cluster is unreachable or authentication was rejected.

    Notes
    -----
    The API key is never included in error messages to prevent secret leakage.
    """
    try:
        info = client.info()
        cluster_name = info.get("cluster_name", "unknown")
        version = info.get("version", {}).get("number", "unknown")
        LOGGER.info(
            "Elasticsearch connected, cluster: '%s', version: %s",
            cluster_name,
            version,
        )
    except ESConnectionError as exc:
        raise ElasticsearchConnectionError(
            f"Cannot connect to Elasticsearch at '{host}'. "
            "Check network connectivity, VPN settings, and cluster health."
        ) from exc
    except TransportError as exc:
        raise ElasticsearchConnectionError(
            f"Elasticsearch at '{host}' rejected the connection. "
            "Verify that the API key is valid and not expired."
        ) from exc



####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\genai\rag\_evaluation.py
####################################################################################################

"""
From-scratch RAG evaluation harness for the YONO Business RAG chatbot.

Pure heuristic / lexical + embedding-cosine-similarity metrics, no
LLM-as-judge, no ``ragas``, no ``datasets``. Intended as an **offline batch
harness** (see ``src/jobs/evaluate_rag_job.py``) run against a golden Q&A
set for regression / CI gating, not as a per-request runtime check.

Metrics
-------
* :func:`faithfulness_score`: stopword-filtered token overlap of the
  answer against the concatenated retrieved context (approximates
  "claims traceable to context").
* :func:`context_precision_score`: average token-overlap of each
  retrieved passage against the ground-truth answer (are the retrieved
  passages relevant to the reference answer?).
* :func:`context_recall_score`: fraction of the ground-truth answer's
  content words covered by the retrieved context as a whole (did
  retrieval surface everything needed?).
* :func:`answer_relevance_score`: cosine similarity between query and
  answer embeddings, reusing the existing :class:`EmbeddingService`
  (``sentence-transformers`` is already an approved dependency).

:class:`RagEvaluator` wraps all four in a
``langchain_core.runnables.RunnableParallel`` so a single ``.evaluate()``
call scores one ``(query, answer, contexts, ground_truth)`` record.
:func:`evaluate_dataset` batches :class:`RagEvaluator` across many records
and aggregates mean/min per metric.
"""

import logging
import re

import numpy as np
from langchain_core.runnables import RunnableLambda, RunnableParallel

from ._embedding_service import EmbeddingService
from ._models import EvaluationReport, EvaluationResult

LOGGER = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")

_WORD_RE = re.compile(r"[a-zA-Z]+")

_STOPWORDS: frozenset[str] = frozenset(
    {
        "the", "a", "an", "is", "are", "was", "were", "be", "been", "being",
        "to", "of", "and", "or", "in", "on", "at", "for", "with", "as",
        "by", "this", "that", "it", "you", "your", "i", "we", "can", "will",
        "please", "from", "if", "not", "no", "do", "does", "did", "have",
        "has", "had", "my", "me", "us", "our",
    }
)


def _tokenize(text: str) -> set[str]:
    return {w.lower() for w in _WORD_RE.findall(text)}


def _content_tokens(text: str) -> set[str]:
    return {t for t in _tokenize(text) if t not in _STOPWORDS and len(t) > 2}


def faithfulness_score(answer: str, contexts: list[str]) -> float:
    """Fraction of the answer's content words traceable to the context.

    Approximates ragas' LLM-graded "faithfulness" metric with a pure
    lexical overlap ratio, no LLM call is made.

    Parameters
    ----------
    answer : str
        The RAG pipeline's generated answer.
    contexts : list[str]
        Retrieved passages that were available to the LLM when generating
        ``answer``.

    Returns
    -------
    float
        Score in ``[0, 1]``. ``1.0`` when the answer has no content words
        (nothing to hallucinate) or when every content word appears in
        the context; lower values indicate ungrounded claims.

    Examples
    --------
    >>> faithfulness_score(
    ...     "Use the YONO app to block your card.",
    ...     ["To block your card, open the YONO app and select Cards."],
    ... ) > 0.5
    True
    """
    answer_tokens = _content_tokens(answer)
    if not answer_tokens:
        return 1.0
    context_tokens = _content_tokens(" ".join(contexts))
    return len(answer_tokens & context_tokens) / len(answer_tokens)


def context_precision_score(contexts: list[str], ground_truth: str) -> float:
    """Average fraction of each retrieved passage relevant to the ground truth.

    Parameters
    ----------
    contexts : list[str]
        Retrieved passages, ranked or unranked.
    ground_truth : str
        SME-authored reference answer for the query.

    Returns
    -------
    float
        Score in ``[0, 1]``. ``0.0`` if there are no contexts or the
        ground truth has no content words.

    Examples
    --------
    >>> context_precision_score(
    ...     ["Block your card via YONO app > Service Requests."],
    ...     "You can block your card using the YONO app.",
    ... ) > 0.0
    True
    """
    ground_truth_tokens = _content_tokens(ground_truth)
    if not contexts or not ground_truth_tokens:
        return 0.0

    precisions: list[float] = []
    for context in contexts:
        context_tokens = _content_tokens(context)
        if not context_tokens:
            precisions.append(0.0)
            continue
        precisions.append(len(context_tokens & ground_truth_tokens) / len(context_tokens))

    return sum(precisions) / len(precisions)


def context_recall_score(contexts: list[str], ground_truth: str) -> float:
    """Fraction of the ground truth's content words covered by retrieval.

    Parameters
    ----------
    contexts : list[str]
        Retrieved passages, ranked or unranked.
    ground_truth : str
        SME-authored reference answer for the query.

    Returns
    -------
    float
        Score in ``[0, 1]``. ``0.0`` if the ground truth has no content
        words or nothing was retrieved.

    Examples
    --------
    >>> context_recall_score(
    ...     ["Block your card via YONO app > Service Requests."],
    ...     "You can block your card using the YONO app.",
    ... ) > 0.0
    True
    """
    ground_truth_tokens = _content_tokens(ground_truth)
    if not ground_truth_tokens:
        return 0.0

    combined_context_tokens: set[str] = set()
    for context in contexts:
        combined_context_tokens |= _content_tokens(context)

    if not combined_context_tokens:
        return 0.0

    return len(combined_context_tokens & ground_truth_tokens) / len(ground_truth_tokens)


def answer_relevance_score(
    query: str,
    answer: str,
    embedding_service: EmbeddingService,
) -> float:
    """Cosine similarity between query and answer embeddings.

    Reuses the existing :class:`EmbeddingService` (embeddings are
    L2-normalised, so the dot product equals cosine similarity).

    Parameters
    ----------
    query : str
        The original user query.
    answer : str
        The RAG pipeline's generated answer.
    embedding_service : EmbeddingService
        Loaded embedding service used to encode both strings.

    Returns
    -------
    float
        Score in ``[0, 1]`` (clamped; raw cosine similarity can be
        slightly negative for unrelated text).

    Examples
    --------
    >>> answer_relevance_score(
    ...     "How do I block my debit card?",
    ...     "Use the YONO app to block your debit card.",
    ...     embedding_service,
    ... ) > 0.5  # doctest: +SKIP
    True
    """
    vectors = embedding_service.encode([query, answer])
    similarity = float(np.dot(vectors[0], vectors[1]))
    return max(0.0, min(1.0, similarity))


class RagEvaluator:
    """Scores a single RAG record across all four heuristic metrics.

    The four metrics are independent of one another, so they are composed
    with ``langchain_core.runnables.RunnableParallel`` rather than a plain
    function call. This keeps the pipeline declarative and makes it easy
    to add or remove metrics without touching call sites.

    Parameters
    ----------
    embedding_service : EmbeddingService
        Loaded embedding service, used only by ``answer_relevance_score``.

    Examples
    --------
    >>> evaluator = RagEvaluator(embedding_service)  # doctest: +SKIP
    >>> result = evaluator.evaluate(
    ...     query="How do I block my debit card?",
    ...     answer="Use the YONO app to block your debit card.",
    ...     contexts=["To block your card, open the YONO app and select Cards."],
    ...     ground_truth="You can block your card using the YONO app.",
    ... )  # doctest: +SKIP
    """

    def __init__(self, embedding_service: EmbeddingService) -> None:
        self.embedding_service = embedding_service
        self._pipeline = RunnableParallel(
            faithfulness=RunnableLambda(
                lambda r: faithfulness_score(r["answer"], r["contexts"])
            ),
            context_precision=RunnableLambda(
                lambda r: context_precision_score(r["contexts"], r["ground_truth"])
            ),
            context_recall=RunnableLambda(
                lambda r: context_recall_score(r["contexts"], r["ground_truth"])
            ),
            answer_relevance=RunnableLambda(
                lambda r: answer_relevance_score(r["query"], r["answer"], self.embedding_service)
            ),
        )

    def evaluate(
        self,
        query: str,
        answer: str,
        contexts: list[str],
        ground_truth: str,
    ) -> EvaluationResult:
        """Score one record and return an :class:`EvaluationResult`.

        Parameters
        ----------
        query : str
            The user query.
        answer : str
            The RAG pipeline's generated answer.
        contexts : list[str]
            Retrieved passages used to ground ``answer``.
        ground_truth : str
            SME-authored reference answer for ``query``.

        Returns
        -------
        EvaluationResult
            Per-metric scores for this record.
        """
        record = {
            "query": query,
            "answer": answer,
            "contexts": contexts,
            "ground_truth": ground_truth,
        }
        scores = self._pipeline.invoke(record)
        return EvaluationResult(
            query=query,
            answer=answer,
            ground_truth=ground_truth,
            **scores,
        )


def evaluate_dataset(
    records: list[dict],
    evaluator: RagEvaluator,
) -> EvaluationReport:
    """Score a batch of RAG records and aggregate mean/min per metric.

    Parameters
    ----------
    records : list[dict]
        Each dict must have keys ``"query"``, ``"answer"``, ``"contexts"``
        (list[str]), and ``"ground_truth"``.
    evaluator : RagEvaluator
        Evaluator instance used to score each record.

    Returns
    -------
    EvaluationReport
        Per-record results plus mean/min aggregates for every metric.

    Raises
    ------
    ValueError
        If ``records`` is empty.

    Examples
    --------
    >>> report = evaluate_dataset(
    ...     [{"query": "q", "answer": "a", "contexts": ["c"], "ground_truth": "g"}],
    ...     evaluator,
    ... )  # doctest: +SKIP
    """
    if not records:
        raise ValueError("evaluate_dataset() received an empty records list.")

    results: list[EvaluationResult] = [
        evaluator.evaluate(
            query=record["query"],
            answer=record["answer"],
            contexts=record["contexts"],
            ground_truth=record["ground_truth"],
        )
        for record in records
    ]

    def _mean_min(attr: str) -> tuple[float, float]:
        values = [getattr(result, attr) for result in results]
        return sum(values) / len(values), min(values)

    faithfulness_mean, faithfulness_min = _mean_min("faithfulness")
    context_precision_mean, context_precision_min = _mean_min("context_precision")
    context_recall_mean, context_recall_min = _mean_min("context_recall")
    answer_relevance_mean, answer_relevance_min = _mean_min("answer_relevance")

    LOGGER.info(
        "RAG evaluation complete: %d records | faithfulness mean=%.3f min=%.3f | "
        "context_precision mean=%.3f min=%.3f | context_recall mean=%.3f min=%.3f | "
        "answer_relevance mean=%.3f min=%.3f",
        len(results),
        faithfulness_mean,
        faithfulness_min,
        context_precision_mean,
        context_precision_min,
        context_recall_mean,
        context_recall_min,
        answer_relevance_mean,
        answer_relevance_min,
    )

    return EvaluationReport(
        results=results,
        faithfulness_mean=faithfulness_mean,
        faithfulness_min=faithfulness_min,
        context_precision_mean=context_precision_mean,
        context_precision_min=context_precision_min,
        context_recall_mean=context_recall_mean,
        context_recall_min=context_recall_min,
        answer_relevance_mean=answer_relevance_mean,
        answer_relevance_min=answer_relevance_min,
    )



####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\genai\rag\_guardrails.py
####################################################################################################

"""
Guardrails service for YONO Business RAG chatbot.

Implements layered input/output validation using only pure heuristics.
No external LLM calls, no NLP model downloads at request time. Every
check is a small, composable function; ``validate_input``/``validate_output``
chain them via ``langchain_core.runnables.RunnableLambda`` into a
declarative pipeline.

* **Query preprocessing**: language detection, short/ambiguous-query
  clarification checks, and acronym/synonym expansion. Runs first, as
  "step 0" of ``validate_input``, before every check below.

* **PII detection and redaction**: regex matchers for Indian banking
  identifiers (PAN, Aadhaar, card numbers, account numbers, phone, email,
  IBAN). Detected entities are replaced with typed placeholders
  (e.g. ``<IN_PAN>``) before the query reaches Elasticsearch or the LLM.

* **Profanity filtering**: ``better-profanity`` word-list check on the
  user query.

* **Toxicity filtering**: ``detoxify`` ("original") toxic-comment
  classifier; queries scoring above ``toxicity_threshold`` are blocked.
  The checkpoint is loaded from a pre-staged HDFS path
  (``detoxify_checkpoint_path``) instead of being downloaded from GitHub
  at runtime, since the bank network has no outbound internet access.

* **Banking-topic enforcement**: a keyword-overlap heuristic that refuses
  queries containing no banking-domain vocabulary, so the assistant only
  engages with YONO / SBI retail-banking questions.

* **Prompt-injection detection**: regex patterns for jailbreak-shaped
  phrasing (role-override, "ignore previous instructions", etc.).

* **Hallucination / grounding check**: a lexical token-overlap ratio
  between the LLM answer and the retrieved context, replacing the old
  LLM-graded grounding check.

* **Regulatory-disclaimer enforcement**: appended automatically whenever
  the answer contains finance-advice keywords.

All public methods are synchronous and side-effect-free on the service
object itself, so the class is safe to share across threads / asyncio
tasks.
"""

import logging
import re
import tempfile
from dataclasses import dataclass

from better_profanity import profanity
from detoxify import Detoxify
from langchain_core.runnables import RunnableLambda

from ...utils import HDFSClient
from ._models import ChatRequest, ChatResponse, SearchHit
from ._preprocessing import _BANKING_KEYWORDS, preprocess_query
from .exceptions import (
    HallucinationDetectedError,
    InvalidCitationError,
    OutOfDomainError,
    PIIDetectedError,
    ProfanityDetectedError,
    PromptInjectionDetectedError,
    RagConfigurationError,
    ToxicContentError,
)

LOGGER = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")

# PII detection - regex matchers, no NLP model needed ------------------------

_PII_PATTERNS: dict[str, re.Pattern[str]] = {
    "IN_PAN": re.compile(r"\b[A-Z]{5}[0-9]{4}[A-Z]\b"),
    "IN_AADHAAR": re.compile(r"\b\d{4}[-\s]?\d{4}[-\s]?\d{4}\b"),
    # 13-19 digit card number, optionally grouped with spaces or dashes.
    "CREDIT_CARD": re.compile(r"\b(?:\d[ -]?){13,19}\b"),
    "PHONE_NUMBER": re.compile(r"\b(?:\+91[-\s]?)?[6-9]\d{9}\b"),
    "EMAIL_ADDRESS": re.compile(r"\b[\w.+-]+@[\w-]+\.[\w.-]+\b"),
    "IBAN_CODE": re.compile(r"\b[A-Z]{2}\d{2}[A-Z0-9]{10,30}\b"),
    # Generic Indian bank account number heuristic (9-18 plain digits).
    "ACCOUNT_NUMBER": re.compile(r"\b\d{9,18}\b"),
}

_DEFAULT_PII_ENTITIES: tuple[str, ...] = (
    "CREDIT_CARD",
    "IN_PAN",
    "IN_AADHAAR",
    "PHONE_NUMBER",
    "EMAIL_ADDRESS",
    "IBAN_CODE",
    "ACCOUNT_NUMBER",
)


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class _PiiMatch:
    entity_type: str
    start: int
    end: int
    text: str


def _detect_pii(text: str, entities: list[str]) -> list[_PiiMatch]:
    """Find non-overlapping PII matches across all requested entity types.

    When two patterns match overlapping spans (for example a card-number
    pattern and the generic account-number pattern both matching the same
    digit run), the longer match wins. Ties are broken by the caller's
    entity priority order.
    """
    candidates: list[_PiiMatch] = []
    for entity_type in entities:
        pattern = _PII_PATTERNS.get(entity_type)
        if pattern is None:
            continue
        for m in pattern.finditer(text):
            candidates.append(_PiiMatch(entity_type, m.start(), m.end(), m.group()))

    priority = {name: i for i, name in enumerate(entities)}
    candidates.sort(key=lambda m: (-len(m.text), priority.get(m.entity_type, len(entities))))

    accepted: list[_PiiMatch] = []
    covered: list[tuple[int, int]] = []
    for m in candidates:
        if any(m.start < e and m.end > s for s, e in covered):
            continue
        accepted.append(m)
        covered.append((m.start, m.end))

    accepted.sort(key=lambda m: m.start)
    return accepted


def _redact_pii(text: str, matches: list[_PiiMatch]) -> str:
    if not matches:
        return text
    parts: list[str] = []
    last = 0
    for m in matches:
        parts.append(text[last : m.start])
        parts.append(f"<{m.entity_type}>")
        last = m.end
    parts.append(text[last:])
    return "".join(parts)


# Prompt-injection detection - regex patterns for jailbreak-style phrasing ---

_INJECTION_PATTERNS: tuple[re.Pattern[str], ...] = tuple(
    re.compile(pattern, re.IGNORECASE)
    for pattern in (
        r"ignore\s+(?:all\s+)?(?:previous|prior|above)\s+instructions",
        r"disregard\s+(?:all\s+)?(?:previous|prior|above)\s+instructions",
        r"pretend\s+(?:you\s+are|to\s+be)",
        r"you\s+are\s+now\s+(?:a|an)\b",
        r"forget\s+(?:you\s+are|your\s+instructions)",
        r"reveal\s+your\s+(?:system\s+)?(?:prompt|instructions|configuration)",
        r"override\s+your\s+instructions",
        r"\bjailbreak\b",
        r"\bDAN\b",
        r"\[\s*system\s*\]",
    )
)

# Banking-topic heuristic - refuse anything with no banking vocabulary -------

_WORD_RE = re.compile(r"[a-zA-Z]+")


def _tokenize(text: str) -> set[str]:
    return {w.lower() for w in _WORD_RE.findall(text)}


# Hallucination and grounding heuristic - lexical overlap of answer vs context

_STOPWORDS: frozenset[str] = frozenset(
    {
        "the", "a", "an", "is", "are", "was", "were", "be", "been", "being",
        "to", "of", "and", "or", "in", "on", "at", "for", "with", "as",
        "by", "this", "that", "it", "you", "your", "i", "we", "can", "will",
        "please", "from", "if", "not", "no", "do", "does", "did", "have",
        "has", "had", "my", "me", "us", "our",
    }
)


def _content_tokens(text: str) -> set[str]:
    return {t for t in _tokenize(text) if t not in _STOPWORDS and len(t) > 2}


def _grounding_score(answer: str, context_text: str) -> float:
    """Fraction of the answer's content words traceable to the context."""
    answer_tokens = _content_tokens(answer)
    if not answer_tokens:
        return 1.0
    context_tokens = _content_tokens(context_text)
    return len(answer_tokens & context_tokens) / len(answer_tokens)


# Keywords that trigger the regulatory disclaimer on LLM output.
_FINANCE_ADVICE_KEYWORDS: tuple[str, ...] = (
    "invest",
    "recommend",
    "should buy",
    "financial advice",
    "portfolio",
    "returns",
)


@dataclass
class _OutputState:
    """Threaded through the output-validation RunnableLambda pipeline."""

    response: ChatResponse
    context_text: str


class GuardrailService:
    """Stateless guardrail service for input/output validation.

    Responsibilities
    ----------------
    - Query preprocessing (language detection, ambiguity check, acronym/
      synonym expansion)
    - PII detection and redaction (regex-based)
    - Profanity filtering (better-profanity)
    - Toxicity filtering (detoxify)
    - Banking-topic enforcement (keyword-overlap heuristic)
    - Prompt-injection detection (regex heuristic)
    - Hallucination detection (lexical context-grounding heuristic)
    - Regulatory disclaimer enforcement

    No external LLM calls are made by this service. Every check is a
    pure, synchronous, deterministic function, which keeps guardrail
    latency negligible relative to the RAG pipeline itself.

    Parameters
    ----------
    pii_entities : list[str], optional
        PII entity types to detect. Defaults to a curated list of Indian
        banking identifiers (PAN, Aadhaar, credit/debit card numbers, etc.).
    enable_preprocessing : bool
        When ``True`` (default) the query is normalised, language-checked,
        and expanded (acronyms/synonyms) before any other guardrail runs.
        Non-English queries raise :class:`~.exceptions.UnsupportedLanguageError`;
        short/ambiguous queries raise :class:`~.exceptions.ClarificationNeededError`.
    enable_pii_redaction : bool
        When ``True`` (default) detected PII is redacted in-place on the
        user query. When ``False`` a :class:`~.exceptions.PIIDetectedError`
        is raised instead so the caller can decide how to handle it.
    enable_profanity_filter : bool
        When ``True`` (default) queries containing profane language are
        blocked with :class:`~.exceptions.ProfanityDetectedError`.
    enable_toxicity_filter : bool
        When ``True`` (default) queries are scored by the ``detoxify``
        toxicity model; scores at or above ``toxicity_threshold`` are
        blocked with :class:`~.exceptions.ToxicContentError`.
    toxicity_threshold : float
        Toxicity score (0-1) at or above which a query is blocked.
        Default is 0.7.
    grounding_threshold : float
        Minimum fraction (0-1) of the answer's content words that must
        overlap with the retrieved context for the answer to be considered
        grounded. Default is 0.15.
    banking_keywords : frozenset[str], optional
        Domain vocabulary used by the banking-topic heuristic. Defaults to
        a curated YONO/SBI retail-banking word list.
    detoxify_checkpoint_path : str, optional
        HDFS path to a pre-staged ``detoxify`` "original" checkpoint
        (``.ckpt`` file). When set, the checkpoint is downloaded from HDFS
        (via :class:`~dlh_ai.utils.HDFSClient`, the same mechanism used for
        the embedding model) instead of ``detoxify`` reaching out to
        GitHub. This is required in the bank network, which has no
        outbound internet access. When ``None``, falls back to
        ``detoxify``'s default GitHub download (only viable in
        environments with internet access, e.g. local development).

    Examples
    --------
    >>> svc = GuardrailService()
    >>> from dlh_ai.genai.rag._models import ChatRequest
    >>> validated = svc.validate_input(ChatRequest(query="What is my balance?"))
    """

    # Construction ------------------------------------------------------

    def __init__(
        self,
        pii_entities: list[str] | None = None,
        enable_preprocessing: bool = True,
        enable_pii_redaction: bool = True,
        enable_profanity_filter: bool = True,
        enable_toxicity_filter: bool = True,
        toxicity_threshold: float = 0.7,
        grounding_threshold: float = 0.15,
        banking_keywords: frozenset[str] | None = None,
        detoxify_checkpoint_path: str | None = None,
    ) -> None:
        LOGGER.info("Loading better-profanity censor word list")
        profanity.load_censor_words()

        self.enable_preprocessing: bool = enable_preprocessing
        self.enable_profanity_filter: bool = enable_profanity_filter
        self.enable_toxicity_filter: bool = enable_toxicity_filter
        self.toxicity_threshold: float = toxicity_threshold
        self.grounding_threshold: float = grounding_threshold

        self._toxicity_model: Detoxify | None = None
        if enable_toxicity_filter:
            self._toxicity_model = self._load_toxicity_model(detoxify_checkpoint_path)

        self.pii_entities: list[str] = pii_entities or list(_DEFAULT_PII_ENTITIES)
        self.enable_pii_redaction: bool = enable_pii_redaction
        self.banking_keywords: frozenset[str] = banking_keywords or _BANKING_KEYWORDS

        LOGGER.info(
            "GuardrailService initialised successfully (heuristic engine, "
            "no LLM calls made by any guardrail check)"
        )

    @staticmethod
    def _load_toxicity_model(checkpoint_path: str | None) -> Detoxify:
        """Load the detoxify "original" checkpoint without reaching GitHub.

        ``Detoxify("original")`` downloads its checkpoint from a GitHub
        release URL on first use, which fails with a DNS/connection error
        in air-gapped bank environments. When *checkpoint_path* is an HDFS
        path, the ``.ckpt`` file is fetched to a local temp file via
        :class:`~dlh_ai.utils.HDFSClient` (mirroring how the embedding
        model is loaded from HDFS) and passed to ``Detoxify`` directly,
        bypassing the download entirely.
        """
        if checkpoint_path:
            LOGGER.info(
                "Loading detoxify toxicity model checkpoint from HDFS: %s",
                checkpoint_path,
            )
            hdfs_client = HDFSClient()
            try:
                with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
                    local_checkpoint = f"{temp_dir}/toxic_original.ckpt"
                    hdfs_client.download(checkpoint_path, local_checkpoint)
                    return Detoxify("original", checkpoint=local_checkpoint)
            except Exception as exc:
                raise RagConfigurationError(
                    f"Failed to load the detoxify checkpoint from HDFS path "
                    f"'{checkpoint_path}': {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}. "
                    "Verify the file has been staged at that path (see the "
                    "'detoxify_checkpoint_path' comment in config.toml for "
                    "the one-time upload steps)."
                ) from exc

        LOGGER.info("Loading detoxify toxicity model ('original')")
        try:
            return Detoxify("original")
        except Exception as exc:
            raise RagConfigurationError(
                "Failed to load the detoxify 'original' toxicity model: "
                f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}. No "
                "'guardrails.detoxify_checkpoint_path' is configured, so "
                "detoxify attempted its default download from GitHub, "
                "which is unreachable from this network. Pre-download "
                "'toxic_original-c1212f89.ckpt' from "
                "https://github.com/unitaryai/detoxify/releases/download/"
                "v0.1-alpha/toxic_original-c1212f89.ckpt on a machine with "
                "internet access, upload it to HDFS (HDFSClient().upload(...)), "
                "and set 'guardrails.detoxify_checkpoint_path' to that HDFS "
                "path, or set 'guardrails.enable_toxicity_filter = false' "
                "to disable this check."
            ) from exc

    # Public API ----------------------------------------------------------

    def validate_input(self, request: ChatRequest) -> ChatRequest:
        """Validate user input before it reaches retrieval.

        Runs a declarative pipeline of pure heuristic checks (no LLM
        calls) in sequence: preprocessing, then profanity, then toxicity,
        then PII, then prompt-injection, then banking-topic enforcement.

        Parameters
        ----------
        request : ChatRequest
            Incoming chat request from the API layer.

        Returns
        -------
        ChatRequest
            Validated request, with PII redacted if ``enable_pii_redaction``
            is ``True``.

        Raises
        ------
        UnsupportedLanguageError
            If the query is written in a non-English script.
        ClarificationNeededError
            If the query is too short or ambiguous to act on directly.
        ProfanityDetectedError
            If profane language is detected in the query.
        ToxicContentError
            If the query is classified as toxic above ``toxicity_threshold``.
        PIIDetectedError
            If PII is found and ``enable_pii_redaction`` is ``False``.
        PromptInjectionDetectedError
            If a prompt-injection / role-override attempt is detected.
        OutOfDomainError
            If the query has no banking-domain vocabulary.
        """
        pipeline = (
            RunnableLambda(self._preprocess)
            | RunnableLambda(self._check_profanity)
            | RunnableLambda(self._check_toxicity)
            | RunnableLambda(self._detect_and_handle_pii)
            | RunnableLambda(self._check_prompt_injection)
            | RunnableLambda(self._check_banking_topic)
        )
        return pipeline.invoke(request)

    def validate_output(
        self,
        response: ChatResponse,
        retrieved_context: list[SearchHit],
    ) -> ChatResponse:
        """Validate LLM output before it is returned to the user.

        Runs a declarative pipeline of pure heuristic checks (no LLM
        calls) in sequence: grounding, then PII leakage, then citation
        accuracy, then regulatory disclaimer.

        Parameters
        ----------
        response : ChatResponse
            LLM-generated response before final delivery.
        retrieved_context : list[SearchHit]
            Ranked knowledge-base passages used to ground the answer.

        Returns
        -------
        ChatResponse
            Validated response, possibly modified (PII redacted, disclaimer
            appended).

        Raises
        ------
        HallucinationDetectedError
            If the answer is detected as hallucinated (not grounded in
            context).
        InvalidCitationError
            If a cited source has no genuine relevance to the query.
        """
        context_text = "\n".join(hit.answer for hit in retrieved_context)
        state = _OutputState(response=response, context_text=context_text)

        pipeline = (
            RunnableLambda(self._check_grounding)
            | RunnableLambda(self._redact_output_pii)
            | RunnableLambda(self._check_citations)
            | RunnableLambda(self._apply_disclaimer)
        )
        result: _OutputState = pipeline.invoke(state)
        return result.response

    # Input-pipeline steps --------------------------------------------------

    def _preprocess(self, request: ChatRequest) -> ChatRequest:
        if not self.enable_preprocessing:
            return request
        return preprocess_query(request)

    def _check_profanity(self, request: ChatRequest) -> ChatRequest:
        if not self.enable_profanity_filter:
            return request
        if profanity.contains_profanity(request.query):
            LOGGER.warning("Profanity detected in user query")
            raise ProfanityDetectedError(
                "Input blocked by guardrails: profane language detected in query"
            )
        return request

    def _check_toxicity(self, request: ChatRequest) -> ChatRequest:
        if not self.enable_toxicity_filter or self._toxicity_model is None:
            return request
        scores: dict[str, float] = self._toxicity_model.predict(request.query)
        toxicity_score = float(scores.get("toxicity", 0.0))
        if toxicity_score >= self.toxicity_threshold:
            LOGGER.warning(
                "Toxic content detected in user query (score=%.4f, threshold=%.4f)",
                toxicity_score,
                self.toxicity_threshold,
            )
            raise ToxicContentError(
                f"Input blocked by guardrails: toxicity score {toxicity_score:.4f} "
                f"exceeds threshold {self.toxicity_threshold:.2f}"
            )
        return request

    def _detect_and_handle_pii(self, request: ChatRequest) -> ChatRequest:
        matches = _detect_pii(request.query, self.pii_entities)
        if not matches:
            return request

        entity_types = [m.entity_type for m in matches]
        LOGGER.warning("PII detected in user query (types: %s)", entity_types)

        if self.enable_pii_redaction:
            redacted = _redact_pii(request.query, matches)
            LOGGER.info("PII redacted from user query")
            return request.model_copy(update={"query": redacted})

        raise PIIDetectedError(f"PII detected in query: {entity_types}")

    def _check_prompt_injection(self, request: ChatRequest) -> ChatRequest:
        if any(pattern.search(request.query) for pattern in _INJECTION_PATTERNS):
            LOGGER.warning(
                "Prompt-injection attempt detected in query: '%s'",
                request.query[:80],
            )
            raise PromptInjectionDetectedError(
                "Input blocked by guardrails (possible prompt injection or "
                "role-override attempt)"
            )
        return request

    def _check_banking_topic(self, request: ChatRequest) -> ChatRequest:
        tokens = _tokenize(request.query)
        if tokens and not (tokens & self.banking_keywords):
            LOGGER.warning(
                "Out-of-domain query blocked by banking-topic guardrail: '%s'",
                request.query[:80],
            )
            raise OutOfDomainError(
                "Input blocked by guardrails (out-of-domain request, please "
                "ask a YONO banking-related question)"
            )
        return request

    # Output-pipeline steps --------------------------------------------------
