    def _check_grounding(self, state: _OutputState) -> _OutputState:
        score = _grounding_score(state.response.answer, state.context_text)
        if score < self.grounding_threshold:
            LOGGER.error(
                "Hallucination detected: grounding score %.4f below threshold %.4f",
                score,
                self.grounding_threshold,
            )
            raise HallucinationDetectedError(
                "Generated answer contains claims not grounded in retrieved "
                f"context (grounding score {score:.4f} below threshold "
                f"{self.grounding_threshold:.2f})"
            )
        return state

    def _redact_output_pii(self, state: _OutputState) -> _OutputState:
        answer = state.response.answer
        matches = _detect_pii(answer, self.pii_entities)
        if not matches:
            return state
        LOGGER.warning(
            "PII detected in LLM output (types: %s); redacting.",
            [m.entity_type for m in matches],
        )
        redacted = _redact_pii(answer, matches)
        state.response = state.response.model_copy(update={"answer": redacted})
        return state

    def _check_citations(self, state: _OutputState) -> _OutputState:
        zero_score_sources = [hit for hit in state.response.sources if hit.score <= 0]
        if zero_score_sources:
            LOGGER.error(
                "Citation validation failed: %d cited source(s) have a "
                "non-positive relevance score (categories: %s)",
                len(zero_score_sources),
                [hit.category for hit in zero_score_sources],
            )
            raise InvalidCitationError(
                "Generated answer cites source(s) with no genuine relevance "
                "to the query"
            )
        return state

    def _apply_disclaimer(self, state: _OutputState) -> _OutputState:
        answer = state.response.answer
        if any(kw in answer.lower() for kw in _FINANCE_ADVICE_KEYWORDS):
            answer += (
                "\n\n**Disclaimer**: This is general information only. "
                "For personalised financial advice, please consult a licensed advisor."
            )
            state.response = state.response.model_copy(update={"answer": answer})
        return state



####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\genai\rag\_ingestion.py
####################################################################################################

"""
Knowledge-base ingestion pipeline for the YONO Business RAG chatbot.

Optimisation highlights (GPU + multi-threading):
* **Vectorised text building**: uses ``pd.Series.str`` ops instead of
  row-by-row ``iterrows()``, which is 10-50× faster on large CSVs.
* **GPU-batched embedding**: delegates to :class:`EmbeddingService` which
  runs on CUDA with FP16 and a configurable ``gpu_batch_size``; the entire
  batch is processed in one GPU kernel launch.
* **Parallel Elasticsearch bulk indexing**: uses ``helpers.parallel_bulk``
  with a thread pool so network I/O and embedding compute overlap.
* **Column validation before any GPU work**: avoids wasting VRAM on bad data.
"""

import logging
from collections.abc import Generator
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
from elasticsearch import Elasticsearch, helpers

from ._embedding_service import EmbeddingService
from ._models import IngestionResult
from .exceptions import (
    ElasticsearchIndexError,
    ElasticsearchIngestionError,
    KnowledgeBaseIngestionError,
)

LOGGER = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")

# Columns that MUST be present in the knowledge-base CSV.
_REQUIRED_COLUMNS: list[str] = [
    "Document-id",
    "Document-type",
    "Document-url",
    "Ingestion-date",
    "Title",
    "Keywords",
    "Category",
    "Sub-category",
    "Serial-no",
    "Question",
    "Solutions",
]


# Index management


def create_or_update_index(
    client: Elasticsearch,
    index_name: str,
    embedding_dimension: int = 384,
    recreate: bool = False,
) -> None:
    """Create the Elasticsearch index with the correct vector mapping.

    If ``recreate=True`` and the index already exists it will be deleted
    first. All existing documents will be lost in that case.

    Parameters
    ----------
    client : Elasticsearch
        Active Elasticsearch client instance.
    index_name : str
        Target index name.
    embedding_dimension : int, optional
        Number of dimensions for the dense_vector field. Must match the
        embedding model output. Default is 384.
    recreate : bool, optional
        If ``True``, delete and recreate the index if it already exists.
        If ``False``, leave the existing index untouched. Default is False,
        since the destructive path (deleting a live index) must be opted
        into explicitly by the caller.

    Raises
    ------
    ElasticsearchIndexError
        Raised when the index creation or deletion operation fails,
        for example due to a mapping conflict or insufficient permissions.

    Notes
    -----
    The ``dims`` value in the mapping is set from ``embedding_dimension``
    at call time, making this function reusable for any vector dimension.

    Examples
    --------
    >>> create_or_update_index(client, "yono_business_knowledge_base", recreate=True)
    """
    # Build mapping dynamically so embedding_dimension is always in sync.
    mappings: dict = {
        "properties": {
            "combined_text": {"type": "text"},
            "combined_text_embedding": {
                "type": "dense_vector",
                "dims": embedding_dimension,
                "index": True,
                "similarity": "cosine",
            },
            "category": {"type": "keyword"},
            "sub_category": {"type": "keyword"},
            "type": {"type": "keyword"},
            "question": {"type": "text"},
            "answer": {"type": "text"},
            "sno": {"type": "integer"},
            "document_id": {"type": "keyword"},
            "document_url": {"type": "keyword"},
            "ingestion_date": {"type": "date"},
            "title": {"type": "text"},
            "keywords": {"type": "text"},
        }
    }

    try:
        index_exists: bool = client.indices.exists(index=index_name)
    except Exception as exc:
        raise ElasticsearchIndexError(
            f"Failed to check existence of Elasticsearch index '{index_name}': "
            f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"
        ) from exc

    if index_exists:
        if recreate:
            LOGGER.warning(
                "Elasticsearch index '%s' already exists and will be deleted (recreate=True).",
                index_name,
            )
            try:
                client.indices.delete(index=index_name)
                LOGGER.info("Existing index '%s' deleted.", index_name)
            except Exception as exc:
                raise ElasticsearchIndexError(
                    f"Failed to delete existing Elasticsearch index '{index_name}': "
                    f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"
                ) from exc
        else:
            LOGGER.info(
                "Elasticsearch index '%s' already exists. Keeping existing index (recreate=False).",
                index_name,
            )
            return

    LOGGER.info("Creating Elasticsearch index '%s' ...", index_name)
    try:
        client.indices.create(index=index_name, mappings=mappings)
    except Exception as exc:
        raise ElasticsearchIndexError(
            f"Failed to create Elasticsearch index '{index_name}': "
            f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"
        ) from exc

    LOGGER.info(
        "Index '%s' created successfully with %s-dimensional vectors.",
        index_name,
        embedding_dimension,
    )


# Text helpers


def _build_actions(
    batch_df: pd.DataFrame,
    embeddings: np.ndarray,
    texts: list[str],
    index_name: str,
) -> Generator[dict[str, object], None, None]:
    """Yield Elasticsearch bulk-action dicts for one embedding batch.

    Defined at module level (not inside the loop) so the function object
    is not re-created on every iteration, which avoids CPython closure overhead
    and keeps the code inspectable / testable.

    Parameters
    ----------
    batch_df : pd.DataFrame
        Slice of the knowledge-base DataFrame for this batch.
    embeddings : np.ndarray
        Float32 array of shape ``(len(batch_df), embedding_dim)``.
    texts : list[str]
        Pre-built ``combined_text`` strings aligned with ``embeddings``.
    index_name : str
        Target Elasticsearch index name.

    Yields
    ------
    dict[str, object]
        One bulk-action document per row.
    """
    for i, (_, row) in enumerate(batch_df.iterrows()):
        yield {
            "_index": index_name,
            "_source": {
                "sno": int(row["Serial-no"]),
                "category": str(row["Category"]).strip(),
                "sub_category": str(row["Sub-category"]).strip(),
                "question": str(row["Question"]).strip(),
                "answer": str(row["Solutions"]).strip(),
                "type": str(row["Document-type"]).strip(),
                "document_id": str(row["Document-id"]).strip(),
                "document_url": str(row["Document-url"]).strip(),
                "ingestion_date": str(row["Ingestion-date"]).strip(),
                "title": str(row["Title"]).strip(),
                "keywords": str(row["Keywords"]).strip(),
                "combined_text": texts[i],
                "combined_text_embedding": embeddings[i].tolist(),
            },
        }


def build_combined_text(row: pd.Series) -> str:
    """Build the text string that will be converted into an embedding vector.

    The combined text concatenates the title, category, sub-category,
    keywords, question, and answer so that the embedding captures all
    contextual signals in a single vector.

    Parameters
    ----------
    row : pandas.Series
        A single row from the knowledge-base DataFrame. Must contain
        ``"Title"``, ``"Category"``, ``"Sub-category"``, ``"Keywords"``,
        ``"Question"``, and ``"Solutions"`` keys.

    Returns
    -------
    str
        Combined text of the form:
        ``"Title: <t>. Category: <cat>. Sub-category: <sub>. Keywords: <kw>.
        Question: <q> Answer: <a>"``.

    Examples
    --------
    >>> import pandas as pd
    >>> row = pd.Series({
    ...     "Title": "yono Business User Registration and Troubleshooting Guide",
    ...     "Category": "Banking",
    ...     "Sub-category": "Digital Access",
    ...     "Keywords": "yono Business; user registration; password reset",
    ...     "Question": "Where can I access yono Business?",
    ...     "Solutions": "Web: https://yonobusiness.sbi.bank.in",
    ... })
    >>> build_combined_text(row)
    'Title: yono Business User Registration and Troubleshooting Guide. Category: Banking. Sub-category: Digital Access. Keywords: yono Business; user registration; password reset. Question: Where can I access yono Business? Answer: Web: https://yonobusiness.sbi.bank.in'
    """
    title: str = str(row.get("Title", "")).strip()
    category: str = str(row.get("Category", "")).strip()
    sub_category: str = str(row.get("Sub-category", "")).strip()
    keywords: str = str(row.get("Keywords", "")).strip()
    question: str = str(row.get("Question", "")).strip()
    answer: str = str(row.get("Solutions", "")).strip()
    return (
        f"Title: {title}. Category: {category}. Sub-category: {sub_category}. "
        f"Keywords: {keywords}. Question: {question} Answer: {answer}"
    )


# Ingestion pipeline


def ingest_knowledge_base(
    client: Elasticsearch,
    embedding_service: EmbeddingService,
    csv_file_path: str | Path,
    index_name: str,
    batch_size: int = 100,
) -> IngestionResult:
    """Read the YONO FAQ CSV, generate embeddings, and bulk-ingest into Elasticsearch.

    The function processes the CSV in configurable batches to bound memory
    usage. Partial batch failures are counted and reported, but they do not
    abort the overall ingestion so that the maximum number of documents are
    indexed even when individual rows fail.

    Parameters
    ----------
    client : Elasticsearch
        Active Elasticsearch client instance.
    embedding_service : EmbeddingService
        Loaded and validated embedding service.
    csv_file_path : str or pathlib.Path
        Path to the knowledge-base CSV file. Must contain columns:
        ``Document-id``, ``Document-type``, ``Document-url``,
        ``Ingestion-date``, ``Title``, ``Keywords``, ``Category``,
        ``Sub-category``, ``Serial-no``, ``Question``, ``Solutions``.
    index_name : str
        Name of the target Elasticsearch index.
    batch_size : int, optional
        Number of rows to embed and index per bulk request. Default is 100.

    Returns
    -------
    IngestionResult
        Summary containing ``total_rows``, ``successful``, and ``failed`` counts.

    Raises
    ------
    KnowledgeBaseIngestionError
        Raised when the CSV file cannot be read or does not contain
        the required columns.
    ElasticsearchIngestionError
        Raised when all batches fail to ingest (i.e., ``successful == 0``
        and ``total_rows > 0``).

    Notes
    -----
    * The ``Category`` and ``Sub-category`` columns are forward-filled
      (``ffill``) before ingestion to handle merged cells from spreadsheet
      exports.
    * ``helpers.bulk`` is called with ``raise_on_error=False`` so partial
      batch failures are counted rather than raising an exception.

    Examples
    --------
    >>> result = ingest_knowledge_base(
    ...     client=es_client,
    ...     embedding_service=emb_service,
    ...     csv_file_path="yono_business_knowledge_base_fill_category.csv",
    ...     index_name="yono_business_knowledge_base",
    ...     batch_size=100,
    ... )
    >>> print(f"Indexed {result.successful}/{result.total_rows} documents.")
    """
    csv_path = Path(csv_file_path)
    LOGGER.info("Reading knowledge-base CSV from: %s", csv_path.resolve())

    if not csv_path.is_file():
        raise KnowledgeBaseIngestionError(
            f"Knowledge-base CSV file not found at '{csv_path.resolve()}'. "
            "Verify 'rag.csv_file_path' in config.toml or the DATA_CSV_PATH "
            "environment variable."
        )

    # Validate required columns from the header alone, before reading any
    # row data, avoiding wasted memory/VRAM on a file with the wrong schema.
    try:
        header_columns: list[str] = list(pd.read_csv(csv_path, nrows=0).columns)
    except Exception as exc:
        raise KnowledgeBaseIngestionError(
            f"Failed to read knowledge-base CSV from '{csv_path}': "
            f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"
        ) from exc

    missing_columns: list[str] = [
        col for col in _REQUIRED_COLUMNS if col not in header_columns
    ]
    if missing_columns:
        raise KnowledgeBaseIngestionError(
            f"Knowledge-base CSV '{csv_path}' is missing required columns: "
            f"{missing_columns}. Available columns: {header_columns}"
        )

    successful: int = 0
    failed: int = 0
    total_rows: int = 0

    # Stream the CSV in fixed-size chunks ------------------------------------
    # Bounds memory to O(batch_size) regardless of file size. A full
    # in-memory pd.read_csv() would OOM on very large knowledge-base exports.
    try:
        chunk_iter = pd.read_csv(csv_path, chunksize=batch_size)
    except Exception as exc:
        raise KnowledgeBaseIngestionError(
            f"Failed to read knowledge-base CSV from '{csv_path}': "
            f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"
        ) from exc

    for batch_num, batch_df in enumerate(chunk_iter, start=1):
        # Normalise missing values before embedding.
        batch_df["Category"] = batch_df["Category"].ffill().fillna("")
        batch_df["Sub-category"] = batch_df["Sub-category"].ffill().fillna("")
        for _col in (
            "Title", "Keywords", "Question", "Solutions",
            "Document-id", "Document-type", "Document-url", "Ingestion-date",
        ):
            batch_df[_col] = batch_df[_col].fillna("")

        # Vectorised combined_text column ---------------------------------
        # Avoid row-by-row Python loop; use pandas str ops (C-level, ~50× faster).
        # Must stay logically identical to build_combined_text() above.
        batch_df["_combined_text"] = (
            "Title: " + batch_df["Title"].str.strip()
            + ". Category: " + batch_df["Category"].str.strip()
            + ". Sub-category: " + batch_df["Sub-category"].str.strip()
            + ". Keywords: " + batch_df["Keywords"].str.strip()
            + ". Question: " + batch_df["Question"].str.strip()
            + " Answer: " + batch_df["Solutions"].str.strip()
        )

        batch_row_start: int = total_rows
        total_rows += len(batch_df)

        LOGGER.info(
            "Embedding batch %d (rows %d–%d)...",
            batch_num,
            batch_row_start,
            total_rows - 1,
        )

        texts: list[str] = batch_df["_combined_text"].tolist()

        # GPU-accelerated embedding: FP16, thread-safe.
        try:
            embeddings: np.ndarray = embedding_service.encode(texts)
        except Exception as exc:
            LOGGER.error(
                "Embedding failed for batch %d, skipping %d documents. "
                "Error: %s",
                batch_num, len(texts), exc,
            )
            failed += len(texts)
            continue

        # parallel_bulk overlaps network I/O with Python processing;
        # thread_count matches Elasticsearch node fanout.
        try:
            for ok, info in helpers.parallel_bulk(
                client,
                _build_actions(batch_df, embeddings, texts, index_name),
                thread_count=4,
                chunk_size=batch_size,
                raise_on_error=False,
            ):
                if ok:
                    successful += 1
                else:
                    failed += 1
                    LOGGER.warning("Document index error: %s", info)
        except Exception as exc:
            LOGGER.error(
                "parallel_bulk failed for batch %d, %d documents lost. "
                "Error: %s",
                batch_num, len(texts), exc,
            )
            failed += len(texts)

    LOGGER.info(
        "Ingestion complete. Total: %d | Successful: %d | Failed: %d",
        total_rows,
        successful,
        failed,
    )

    if total_rows > 0 and successful == 0:
        raise ElasticsearchIngestionError(
            f"Ingestion of '{csv_path}' into index '{index_name}' produced "
            f"zero successful documents out of {total_rows}. "
            "Check Elasticsearch cluster health and index mapping."
        )

    return IngestionResult(
        total_rows=total_rows,
        successful=successful,
        failed=failed,
    )



####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\genai\rag\_llm_service.py
####################################################################################################

"""
LLM provider service for the YONO Business RAG chatbot.

Uses the DLH self-hosted vLLM OpenAI-compatible inference endpoint
(e.g. Gemma-4-26b-a4b-it) via the standard ``openai`` Python client.

Optimisation highlights (thousands of concurrent users):
* **Persistent connection pool**: a single ``httpx.Client`` is created
  once and reused across all calls, eliminating per-request TCP handshake
  overhead.  Pool size is tuned via ``connection_pool_size``.
* **Retry with exponential back-off**: transient 5xx errors and network
  blips are retried up to ``max_retries`` times with jittered delays so
  a sudden spike does not cascade into a storm of failed requests.
* **Async-friendly**: ``generate()`` is synchronous (called via
  ``run_in_executor`` from FastAPI) so it never blocks the event loop;
  the underlying ``httpx.Client`` handles connection reuse at the OS level.
* **Keep-alive headers**: ensures the vLLM server keeps connections open
  between requests, saving 1-3 ms per call.
"""

import logging
import random
import time
from dataclasses import dataclass, field

import httpx
from openai import APIConnectionError, APIStatusError, OpenAI

from .exceptions import LlmGenerationError, LlmLoadError

LOGGER = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")

# Sentinel value for the DLH self-hosted endpoint that uses no real key.
_DLH_EMPTY_KEY: str = "EMPTY"

# Retry config
_RETRY_BASE_DELAY_S: float = 0.1
_RETRY_MAX_DELAY_S: float = 2.0


@dataclass
class LlmService:
    """Manages the lifecycle of the DLH self-hosted LLM client.

    Uses the DLH vLLM OpenAI-compatible endpoint.  The ``httpx.Client``
    and ``OpenAI`` wrapper are initialised once via :meth:`load` and
    reused for all subsequent :meth:`generate` calls, providing
    connection-pool reuse across thousands of concurrent requests.

    Parameters
    ----------
    model_name : str
        Model identifier sent in every completion request.
        Example: ``"gemma-4-26b-a4b-it"``.
    base_url : str
        Base URL for the DLH vLLM OpenAI-compatible inference server.
    api_key : str
        API key for the DLH endpoint. Use ``"EMPTY"`` for unauthenticated
        endpoints.
    verify_ssl : bool
        Whether to verify the TLS certificate. Default is ``True``. Only
        set to False for a UAT environment with self-signed certificates.
    temperature : float
        Sampling temperature. Range [0.0, 2.0]. Default is 0.2.
    max_tokens : int
        Maximum tokens in the generated response. Default is 1024.
    request_timeout : float
        HTTP timeout in seconds per LLM call. Default is 60.0.
    connection_pool_size : int
        Maximum number of keep-alive HTTP connections to the vLLM server.
        Should be >= the number of Uvicorn/gunicorn worker threads that
        can call :meth:`generate` concurrently. Default is 100.
    max_retries : int
        Number of retries on transient errors (5xx, connection reset, 429).
        Default is 3.
    use_max_completion_tokens : bool
        Whether to send ``max_completion_tokens`` (newer OpenAI Chat
        Completions spec) instead of the legacy ``max_tokens`` parameter.
        Some self-hosted vLLM builds only understand the legacy name.
        Default is ``False`` (send ``max_tokens``, the widest-compatible
        option for vLLM).
    """

    model_name: str
    base_url: str
    api_key: str
    verify_ssl: bool = True
    temperature: float = 0.2
    max_tokens: int = 1024
    request_timeout: float = 60.0
    connection_pool_size: int = 100
    max_retries: int = 3
    use_max_completion_tokens: bool = False

    _client: OpenAI | None = field(default=None, init=False, repr=False)
    _http_client: httpx.Client | None = field(default=None, init=False, repr=False)
    _send_sampling_params: bool = field(default=True, init=False, repr=False)

    # Public lifecycle ---------------------------------------------------

    def load(self) -> None:
        """Initialise the DLH OpenAI-compatible client with connection pooling.

        Must be called once before any :meth:`generate` call.

        Raises
        ------
        LlmLoadError
            If ``base_url`` is empty or the ``OpenAI`` constructor fails.
        """
        self._load_dlh_client()

    def generate(self, system_prompt: str, user_prompt: str) -> str:
        """Send a prompt to the LLM and return the generated text.

        Retries up to ``max_retries`` times on transient errors with
        exponential back-off + jitter.

        Parameters
        ----------
        system_prompt : str
            System-role instruction that sets the assistant's persona.
        user_prompt : str
            User-role message with the RAG context and the customer's question.

        Returns
        -------
        str
            Generated answer text, stripped of leading/trailing whitespace.

        Raises
        ------
        LlmLoadError
            If :meth:`load` has not been called.
        LlmGenerationError
            If the LLM endpoint fails after all retries.
        """
        if self._client is None:
            raise LlmLoadError(
                "LlmService.generate() called before LlmService.load(). "
                "Call load() once during application startup."
            )

        LOGGER.debug(
            "Sending completion request to model '%s' (base_url=%s).",
            self.model_name,
            self.base_url,
        )

        messages: list[dict[str, str]] = [
            {"role": "system", "content": system_prompt},
            {"role": "user",   "content": user_prompt},
        ]

        # `max_tokens` was renamed to `max_completion_tokens` in the newer
        # OpenAI Chat Completions spec. Some self-hosted vLLM builds only
        # understand the legacy `max_tokens` name, so which one is sent is
        # configurable via `use_max_completion_tokens`.
        completion_kwargs: dict[str, object] = {
            "model": self.model_name,
            "messages": messages,
        }
        if self.use_max_completion_tokens:
            completion_kwargs["max_completion_tokens"] = self.max_tokens
        else:
            completion_kwargs["max_tokens"] = self.max_tokens
        if self._send_sampling_params:
            completion_kwargs["temperature"] = self.temperature

        last_exc: Exception | None = None
        for attempt in range(1, self.max_retries + 2):  # +2 covers the first try plus retries
            try:
                response = self._client.chat.completions.create(**completion_kwargs)
                break  # Success
            except APIConnectionError as exc:
                last_exc = exc
                if attempt > self.max_retries:
                    raise LlmGenerationError(
                        "LLM endpoint is unreachable after "
                        f"{self.max_retries} retries. "
                        f"Base URL: '{self.base_url}'."
                    ) from exc
                delay = min(
                    _RETRY_BASE_DELAY_S * (2 ** (attempt - 1)) + random.uniform(0, 0.05),
                    _RETRY_MAX_DELAY_S,
                )
                LOGGER.warning(
                    "LLM connection error (attempt %d/%d). Retrying in %.2fs: %s",
                    attempt, self.max_retries + 1, delay, exc,
                )
                time.sleep(delay)
            except APIStatusError as exc:
                # Retry on server-side errors (5xx) and 429 (rate limit);
                # propagate other 4xx errors immediately, they will not
                # resolve on retry (bad request, auth failure, etc.).
                is_retryable: bool = exc.status_code >= 500 or exc.status_code == 429
                if not is_retryable:
                    raise LlmGenerationError(
                        f"LLM endpoint returned HTTP {exc.status_code}. "
                        "Verify the API key and model name are correct."
                    ) from exc
                last_exc = exc
                if attempt > self.max_retries:
                    raise LlmGenerationError(
                        f"LLM endpoint returned HTTP {exc.status_code} after "
                        f"{self.max_retries} retries. Last error: {last_exc}"
                    ) from exc
                # Respect a server-supplied Retry-After header (common on 429)
                # when present, otherwise fall back to exponential backoff.
                retry_after: str | None = exc.response.headers.get("retry-after") if exc.response else None
                if retry_after is not None:
                    try:
                        delay = min(float(retry_after), _RETRY_MAX_DELAY_S * 10)
                    except ValueError:
                        delay = _RETRY_MAX_DELAY_S
                else:
                    delay = min(
                        _RETRY_BASE_DELAY_S * (2 ** (attempt - 1)) + random.uniform(0, 0.05),
                        _RETRY_MAX_DELAY_S,
                    )
                LOGGER.warning(
                    "LLM HTTP %d error (attempt %d/%d). Retrying in %.2fs.",
                    exc.status_code, attempt, self.max_retries + 1, delay,
                )
                time.sleep(delay)
            except Exception as exc:
                raise LlmGenerationError(
                    f"Unexpected error during LLM generation for model "
                    f"'{self.model_name}': {type(exc).__name__} - {exc}"
                ) from exc

        try:
            answer: str = response.choices[0].message.content or ""
        except (IndexError, AttributeError) as exc:
            raise LlmGenerationError(
                f"LLM response from model '{self.model_name}' had an unexpected "
                "structure. Expected at least one choice with a message content field."
            ) from exc

        LOGGER.debug(
            "LLM generation complete. Response length: %d chars.", len(answer)
        )
        return answer.strip()

    def shutdown(self) -> None:
        """Close the underlying HTTP connection pool.

        Call this from the FastAPI lifespan shutdown handler.
        """
        if self._http_client is not None:
            try:
                self._http_client.close()
                LOGGER.info("LLM HTTP connection pool closed.")
            except Exception:
                pass

    # Private loader -------------------------------------------------------

    def _load_dlh_client(self) -> None:
        """Initialise the DLH vLLM OpenAI-compatible client with a pooled httpx transport.

        Raises
        ------
        LlmLoadError
            If ``base_url`` is empty or the ``OpenAI`` constructor fails.
        """
        if not self.base_url:
            raise LlmLoadError(
                "DLH base URL is empty. "
                "Set 'LLM__BASE_URL' in the environment or config.toml."
            )

        if not self.verify_ssl:
            LOGGER.warning(
                "LLM client: TLS certificate verification is DISABLED. "
                "This must only be used in UAT with self-signed certificates, "
                "set LLM__VERIFY_SSL=true in production."
            )

        # Build a persistent httpx.Client with an explicit connection pool.
        # max_connections controls keep-alive pool size; limits concurrent
        # requests to prevent overwhelming the vLLM server.
        transport = httpx.HTTPTransport(
            verify=self.verify_ssl,
            retries=0,  # We handle retries ourselves above for full control.
        )
        self._http_client = httpx.Client(
            transport=transport,
            verify=self.verify_ssl,
            limits=httpx.Limits(
                max_connections=self.connection_pool_size,
                max_keepalive_connections=self.connection_pool_size,
                keepalive_expiry=30.0,
            ),
            headers={"Connection": "keep-alive"},
        )

        try:
            self._client = OpenAI(
                api_key=self.api_key or _DLH_EMPTY_KEY,
                base_url=self.base_url,
                timeout=self.request_timeout,
                http_client=self._http_client,
            )
            LOGGER.info(
                "DLH LLM client initialised, model: '%s' | base_url: '%s' | "
                "pool_size: %d | retries: %d",
                self.model_name,
                self.base_url,
                self.connection_pool_size,
                self.max_retries,
            )
        except Exception as exc:
            raise LlmLoadError(
                f"Failed to initialise DLH OpenAI-compatible client "
                f"at '{self.base_url}': {type(exc).__name__} - {exc}"
            ) from exc



####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\genai\rag\_models.py
####################################################################################################

"""
Pydantic models / data-transfer objects for the YONO Business RAG pipeline.

Every boundary in the pipeline (Elasticsearch documents, API request /
response payloads, search results) is typed with a Pydantic model so
that all data is validated and serialised consistently.

The models deliberately avoid ``Any`` and use only concrete, narrow types.
"""

from pydantic import BaseModel, ConfigDict, Field


# Elasticsearch document


class KnowledgeBaseDocument(BaseModel):
    """Represents a single document stored in the Elasticsearch index.

    This model mirrors the Elasticsearch mapping defined for the
    ``yono_business_knowledge_base`` index.

    Attributes
    ----------
    sno : int
        Sequential row number from the original knowledge-base CSV
        (source column: ``Serial-no``).
    category : str
        YONO service category (e.g., "ATM/Debit Card Service").
    question : str
        The FAQ question text.
    answer : str
        The FAQ answer text (source column: ``Solutions``).
    type : str
        Document classification. Now sourced from the CSV's
        ``Document-type`` column (e.g., "xlsx" - the source file format),
        not a FAQ-topic classifier.
    combined_text : str
        Concatenated Title + Category + Sub-category + Keywords + Question
        + Answer used for embedding.
    combined_text_embedding : list[float]
        384-dimensional normalised cosine-similarity vector.
    document_id : str | None
        Source document UUID (CSV column: ``Document-id``). Provenance only.
    document_url : str | None
        Relative path/URL to the original source document (CSV column:
        ``Document-url``). Display/citation only.
    ingestion_date : str | None
        ISO date string the source document was ingested (CSV column:
        ``Ingestion-date``).
    title : str | None
        SME-authored title of the source document (CSV column: ``Title``).
    keywords : str | None
        ``;``-separated lookup keywords (CSV column: ``Keywords``).
    sub_category : str | None
        Finer-grained classification within ``category`` (CSV column:
        ``Sub-category``).

    Examples
    --------
    >>> doc = KnowledgeBaseDocument(
    ...     sno=1,
    ...     category="Banking",
    ...     question="Where can I access yono Business?",
    ...     answer="Web: https://yonobusiness.sbi.bank.in ...",
    ...     type="xlsx",
    ...     combined_text="Title: ... Category: Banking. ...",
    ...     combined_text_embedding=[0.01] * 384,
    ...     document_id="afdb20fd-5b40-5f14-a8fa-b3aa3d45a4a2",
    ...     document_url="docs/Questionnaire final.xlsx",
    ...     ingestion_date="2026-09-08",
    ...     title="yono Business User Registration and Troubleshooting Guide",
    ...     keywords="yono Business; user registration; password reset",
    ...     sub_category="Digital Access",
    ... )
    """

    model_config = ConfigDict(frozen=True)

    sno: int
    category: str
    question: str
    answer: str
    type: str
    combined_text: str
    combined_text_embedding: list[float] = Field(default_factory=list)
    document_id: str | None = None
    document_url: str | None = None
    ingestion_date: str | None = None
    title: str | None = None
    keywords: str | None = None
    sub_category: str | None = None


# Individual search hit returned by Elasticsearch


class SearchHit(BaseModel):
    """A single ranked result from a kNN vector search.

    Attributes
    ----------
    score : float
        Cosine-similarity score in the range [0, 1]. Higher is more relevant.
    sno : int
        Sequential row number from the knowledge-base.
    category : str
        YONO service category for this document.
    question : str
        The FAQ question text.
    answer : str
        The FAQ answer text.
    doc_type : str
        Document classification label. Now sourced from the CSV's
        ``Document-type`` column (e.g., "xlsx" - the source file format),
        not a FAQ-topic classifier.
    document_id : str | None
        Source document UUID. Provenance only.
    document_url : str | None
        Relative path/URL to the original source document. Display/citation
        only.
    ingestion_date : str | None
        ISO date string the source document was ingested.
    title : str | None
        SME-authored title of the source document.
    keywords : str | None
        ``;``-separated lookup keywords.
    sub_category : str | None
        Finer-grained classification within ``category``.

    Examples
    --------
    >>> hit = SearchHit(
    ...     score=0.9477,
    ...     sno=1004,
    ...     category="Banking",
    ...     question="How can I enquire about past transactions?",
    ...     answer="Click on each account under 'My Borrowings'.",
    ...     doc_type="xlsx",
    ... )
    """

    model_config = ConfigDict(frozen=True)

    score: float
    sno: int
    category: str
    question: str
    answer: str
    doc_type: str
    document_id: str | None = None
    document_url: str | None = None
    ingestion_date: str | None = None
    title: str | None = None
    keywords: str | None = None
    sub_category: str | None = None


# Vector search response


class VectorSearchResponse(BaseModel):
    """Encapsulates the full response of a kNN vector search.

    Attributes
    ----------
    query : str
        The original user query that produced this response.
    total_hits : int
        Total number of documents returned by Elasticsearch.
    hits : list[SearchHit]
        Ranked list of matching knowledge-base documents.

    Examples
    --------
    >>> resp = VectorSearchResponse(
    ...     query="How do I activate my debit card?",
    ...     total_hits=5,
    ...     hits=[],
    ... )
    """

    model_config = ConfigDict(frozen=True)

    query: str
    total_hits: int
    hits: list[SearchHit] = Field(default_factory=list)


# Chat API request / response


class ChatRequest(BaseModel):
    """Incoming chat message payload from the frontend.

    Attributes
    ----------
    query : str
        The user's natural-language question.
    top_k : int
        Maximum number of knowledge-base documents to retrieve.
        Must be between 1 and 20 (inclusive). Defaults to 5.
    session_id : str | None
        Optional session identifier for conversation tracking.

    Examples
    --------
    >>> req = ChatRequest(query="How do I block my debit card?", top_k=5)
    """

    query: str = Field(..., min_length=1, max_length=2000)
    top_k: int = Field(default=5, ge=1, le=20)
    session_id: str | None = Field(default=None)


class ChatResponse(BaseModel):
    """Response payload returned to the frontend after a RAG lookup.

    Attributes
    ----------
    query : str
        Echo of the original user query.
    answer : str
        Best-match answer from the knowledge base.
    category : str
        YONO category of the top-ranked result.
    doc_type : str
        Type label of the top-ranked result.
    score : float
        Cosine similarity score of the top-ranked result.
    sources : list[SearchHit]
        All retrieved supporting documents for transparency.
    session_id : str | None
        Echoed session identifier if provided in the request.
    source_table_link : str | None
        Optional link to the source-of-truth table row for the top-ranked
        source (built from ``[rag].source_table_url_template``). ``None``
        when the feature is disabled via config.

    Examples
    --------
    >>> resp = ChatResponse(
    ...     query="How do I block my debit card?",
    ...     answer="Temporary block your card via YONO app ...",
    ...     category="ATM/Debit Card Service",
    ...     doc_type="Main Category",
    ...     score=0.94,
    ...     sources=[],
    ...     session_id=None,
    ... )
    """

    query: str
    answer: str
    category: str
    doc_type: str
    score: float
    sources: list[SearchHit] = Field(default_factory=list)
    session_id: str | None = None
    source_table_link: str | None = None


# RAG evaluation (from-scratch heuristic harness)


class EvaluationResult(BaseModel):
    """Per-record RAG evaluation scores (from-scratch heuristic harness).

    Attributes
    ----------
    query : str
        The evaluated user query.
    answer : str
        The RAG pipeline's generated answer.
    ground_truth : str
        SME-authored reference answer for this query.
    faithfulness : float
        Fraction of the answer's content words traceable to retrieved
        context (0-1). Higher is better; low values suggest hallucination.
    context_precision : float
        Average fraction of each retrieved passage's content words that
        also appear in the ground-truth answer (0-1).
    context_recall : float
        Fraction of the ground-truth answer's content words covered by
        the retrieved context as a whole (0-1).
    answer_relevance : float
        Cosine similarity between query and answer embeddings (0-1).

    Examples
    --------
    >>> result = EvaluationResult(
    ...     query="How do I block my debit card?",
    ...     answer="Use the YONO app under Service Requests.",
    ...     ground_truth="Block your card via YONO app > Service Requests.",
    ...     faithfulness=0.82,
    ...     context_precision=0.75,
    ...     context_recall=0.68,
    ...     answer_relevance=0.91,
    ... )
    """

    model_config = ConfigDict(frozen=True)

    query: str
    answer: str
    ground_truth: str
    faithfulness: float
    context_precision: float
    context_recall: float
    answer_relevance: float


class EvaluationReport(BaseModel):
    """Aggregate report over a batch of :class:`EvaluationResult` records.

    Attributes
    ----------
    results : list[EvaluationResult]
        Per-record scores for every evaluated query.
    faithfulness_mean : float
        Mean faithfulness score across all records.
    faithfulness_min : float
        Minimum faithfulness score across all records.
    context_precision_mean : float
        Mean context-precision score across all records.
    context_precision_min : float
        Minimum context-precision score across all records.
    context_recall_mean : float
        Mean context-recall score across all records.
    context_recall_min : float
        Minimum context-recall score across all records.
    answer_relevance_mean : float
        Mean answer-relevance score across all records.
    answer_relevance_min : float
        Minimum answer-relevance score across all records.

    Examples
    --------
    >>> report = EvaluationReport(
    ...     results=[],
    ...     faithfulness_mean=0.0,
    ...     faithfulness_min=0.0,
    ...     context_precision_mean=0.0,
    ...     context_precision_min=0.0,
    ...     context_recall_mean=0.0,
    ...     context_recall_min=0.0,
    ...     answer_relevance_mean=0.0,
    ...     answer_relevance_min=0.0,
    ... )
    """

    model_config = ConfigDict(frozen=True)

    results: list[EvaluationResult] = Field(default_factory=list)
    faithfulness_mean: float
    faithfulness_min: float
    context_precision_mean: float
    context_precision_min: float
    context_recall_mean: float
    context_recall_min: float
    answer_relevance_mean: float
    answer_relevance_min: float


# Ingestion statistics


class IngestionResult(BaseModel):
    """Summary of a knowledge-base ingestion run.

    Attributes
    ----------
    total_rows : int
        Total rows read from the source CSV.
    successful : int
        Documents successfully indexed in Elasticsearch.
    failed : int
        Documents that could not be indexed due to errors.

    Examples
    --------
    >>> result = IngestionResult(total_rows=1039, successful=1039, failed=0)
    >>> assert result.failed == 0
    """

    model_config = ConfigDict(frozen=True)

    total_rows: int
    successful: int
    failed: int



####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\genai\rag\_preprocessing.py
####################################################################################################

"""
Query preprocessing for the YONO Business RAG chatbot.

Runs as step 0 of ``GuardrailService.validate_input``, before profanity/
PII/injection/topic - a short, ambiguous, non-English, or acronym-heavy
query fails those later checks (or retrieval) for reasons that have
nothing to do with the query's actual content. Everything here is a
regex match, dict lookup, or Unicode range check: no ML models, no
network calls.
"""

import logging
import re
import unicodedata

from ._models import ChatRequest
from .exceptions import ClarificationNeededError, UnsupportedLanguageError

logger = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")


# Banking-domain vocabulary - also used by GuardrailService's own
# banking-topic guardrail (see _guardrails.py: _check_banking_topic), so
# it lives here and gets imported rather than kept as two word lists that
# could drift apart.
_BANKING_KEYWORDS: frozenset[str] = frozenset(
    {
        "yono", "sbi", "bank", "banking", "account", "accounts", "savings",
        "current", "balance", "deposit", "deposits", "withdraw", "withdrawal",
        "transfer", "neft", "rtgs", "imps", "upi", "ifsc", "branch", "atm",
        "debit", "credit", "card", "cards", "cvv", "pin", "otp", "kyc",
        "loan", "loans", "emi", "interest", "fd", "rd", "ppf", "nominee",
        "nomination", "netbanking", "internet", "mobile", "app", "passbook",
        "statement", "statements", "cheque", "chequebook", "draft", "swift",
        "iban", "aadhaar", "pan", "insurance", "premium", "mutual", "fund",
        "funds", "investment", "tax", "gst", "remittance", "forex", "locker",
        "overdraft", "minimum", "complaint", "grievance", "activate",
        "block", "unblock", "transaction", "transactions", "payee",
        "beneficiary", "wallet", "merchant", "qr", "pos", "recharge", "bill",
        "payment", "payments", "pay", "limit", "charges", "charge", "fee",
        "fees", "customer", "care", "helpline", "service", "services",
        "request", "application", "apply", "renew", "renewal", "reset",
        "update", "register", "registration", "login", "password", "mpin",
        "biometric",
    }
)

_ACRONYM_MAP: dict[str, str] = {
    "fd": "fixed deposit", "rd": "recurring deposit",
    "ppf": "public provident fund", "sb": "savings bank account",
    "ca": "current account", "mab": "monthly average balance",
    "neft": "national electronic funds transfer",
    "rtgs": "real time gross settlement",
    "imps": "immediate payment service", "upi": "unified payments interface",
    "nach": "national automated clearing house",
    "ecs": "electronic clearing service",
    "kyc": "know your customer", "aml": "anti money laundering",
    "otp": "one time password", "mpin": "mobile pin",
    "cvv": "card verification value", "ifsc": "indian financial system code",
    "pan": "permanent account number",
    "emi": "equated monthly instalment", "gst": "goods and services tax",
    "dd": "demand draft", "od": "overdraft",
}

_SYNONYM_MAP: dict[str, str] = {
    "forgot password": "forgot password reset credentials",
    "reset password": "reset password credentials",
    "change password": "change password credentials",
    "password not working": "password issue reset credentials",
    "cannot login": "login issue password credentials",
    "can't login": "login issue password credentials",
    "unable to login": "login issue password credentials",
    "login problem": "login issue password credentials",
    "authorise": "authorise authorize authenticate",
    "authorize": "authorise authorize authenticate",
    "authentication": "authorise authenticate",
}

# (pattern, clarification question) - first match wins.  A `None` question
# means "this looks fine as-is, don't ask for clarification" (currently
# only the greeting pattern) and must be checked before the generic
# short-query catch-all below it.
_CLARIFICATION_TRIGGERS: list[tuple[str, str | None]] = [
    (r"\b(forgot|forget|reset|change|lost)\s+(my\s+)?"
     r"(password|credentials|login|mpin|pin|id|user\s*id|username)\b",
     "Which would you like to reset - your YONO Business login password, "
     "MPIN, or ATM/Debit card PIN?"),
    (r"\b(not\s+working|not\s+loading|error|issue|problem|trouble|failing)\b",
     "Could you tell me which feature or transaction is not working? "
     "For example - login, fund transfer, card, or FD/RD?"),
    (r"^\s*(hi|hello|hey|good\s+morning|good\s+afternoon|good\s+evening|"
     r"namaste)\s*\.?\s*$", None),
    (r"\b(help|assist|support)\s*\??\s*$",
     "Of course! Could you tell me what you need help with? For example "
     "- account services, cards, loans, or FD/RD?"),
    (r"^\s*\w{1,3}\s*$",
     "Could you provide a bit more detail about what you'd like to know?"),
]

# Queries matching any of these are never ambiguous even if short - "how do
# I open an FD" shouldn't be treated the same as a bare "FD".
_CLARIFICATION_EXCEPTIONS: tuple[str, ...] = (
    r"\b(how|what|when|where|why|steps|process|procedure)\b",
    r"\b(enable|disable|activate|deactivate)\b",
    r"\b(limit|charge|fee|rate)\b",
)

_SHORT_QUERY_WORD_THRESHOLD = 10

# Words that mean "this query already has enough context, don't add more".
_DOMAIN_SIGNAL_WORDS: tuple[str, ...] = ("yono", "sbi", "business")

# Non-English Unicode block ranges - zero-dependency language detection.
_NON_ENGLISH_RANGES: tuple[tuple[int, int, str], ...] = (
    (0x0900, 0x097F, "Hindi (Devanagari)"), (0x0980, 0x09FF, "Bengali"),
    (0x0A00, 0x0A7F, "Punjabi (Gurmukhi)"), (0x0A80, 0x0AFF, "Gujarati"),
    (0x0B00, 0x0B7F, "Odia"), (0x0B80, 0x0BFF, "Tamil"),
    (0x0C00, 0x0C7F, "Telugu"), (0x0C80, 0x0CFF, "Kannada"),
    (0x0D00, 0x0D7F, "Malayalam"), (0x0600, 0x06FF, "Arabic/Urdu"),
    (0x4E00, 0x9FFF, "Chinese"), (0x3040, 0x309F, "Japanese Hiragana"),
    (0xAC00, 0xD7AF, "Korean"),
)

_NON_ENGLISH_MESSAGE = (
    "I currently support queries only in English. Support for {language} "
    "will be added soon. Please type your question in English and I will "
    "be happy to assist you."
)


def _detect_language(text: str) -> str | None:
    """Return the name of the first non-English script found in *text*, if any."""
    for char in text:
        cp = ord(char)
        for start, end, lang in _NON_ENGLISH_RANGES:
            if start <= cp <= end:
                return lang
    return None


def _normalise_special_chars(text: str) -> str:
    """Fold Unicode dash/quote variants and stray "word*word" asterisks to a consistent form."""
    text = unicodedata.normalize("NFKC", text)
    text = re.sub(r"[–-−‐‑‒]", "-", text)
    text = re.sub(r"(?<=[A-Za-z])\*(?=[A-Za-z])", "-", text)
    text = text.replace("‘", "'").replace("’", "'")
    text = text.replace("“", '"').replace("”", '"')
    text = re.sub(r"\s+", " ", text)
    return text.strip()


def _check_needs_clarification(text: str) -> str | None:
    """Return a clarification question if *text* is too ambiguous to act on, else None."""
    text_lower = text.lower().strip()
    if any(
        re.search(pattern, text_lower, re.IGNORECASE)
        for pattern in _CLARIFICATION_EXCEPTIONS
    ):
        return None
    for pattern, question in _CLARIFICATION_TRIGGERS:
        if re.search(pattern, text_lower, re.IGNORECASE):
            return question
    return None


def _expand_acronyms(text: str) -> str:
    """Expand whole-word banking acronyms (longest key first) unless already spelled out."""
    result = text
    for acronym in sorted(_ACRONYM_MAP, key=len, reverse=True):
        expansion = _ACRONYM_MAP[acronym]
        if expansion.lower() in result.lower():
            continue
        pattern = rf"\b{re.escape(acronym)}\b"
        if re.search(pattern, result, re.IGNORECASE):
            result = re.sub(pattern, expansion, result, flags=re.IGNORECASE)
    return result


def _expand_synonyms(text: str) -> str:
    """Rewrite common troubleshooting phrases (longest key first) onto a shared vocabulary."""
    result = text
    for phrase in sorted(_SYNONYM_MAP, key=len, reverse=True):
        pattern = re.escape(phrase)
        if re.search(pattern, result, re.IGNORECASE):
            result = re.sub(pattern, _SYNONYM_MAP[phrase], result, flags=re.IGNORECASE)
    return result


def _add_implicit_domain_context(text: str) -> str:
    """Ready-to-enable hook for prepending domain context to short, bare queries.

    Currently a no-op - every branch below falls through to returning
    *text* unchanged.  Kept fully wired (word-count / banking-noun /
    domain-signal checks) so flipping the final line on is a one-line
    change if implicit-context injection turns out to be wanted.
    """
    words = text.lower().split()
    if len(words) >= _SHORT_QUERY_WORD_THRESHOLD:
        return text
    if not any(w in _BANKING_KEYWORDS for w in words):
        return text
    if any(signal in text.lower() for signal in _DOMAIN_SIGNAL_WORDS):
        return text
    # return f"{text} in YONO Business"
    return text


def preprocess_query(request: ChatRequest) -> ChatRequest:
    """Normalise, validate, and expand ``request.query`` before any other guardrail runs.

    Parameters
    ----------
    request : ChatRequest
        Incoming chat request, not yet touched by any guardrail.

    Returns
    -------
    ChatRequest
        *request* unchanged if nothing needed rewriting, otherwise a copy
        with ``query`` normalised/expanded.

    Raises
    ------
    UnsupportedLanguageError
        The query is written in a non-English script.
    ClarificationNeededError
        The query is too short or ambiguous to act on directly.
    """
    # Non-English input can't be answered against an English-only FAQ
    # base - block it before spending a retrieval + LLM call on it.
    language = _detect_language(request.query)
    if language:
        logger.info("Non-English query blocked (detected: %s)", language)
        raise UnsupportedLanguageError(
            _NON_ENGLISH_MESSAGE.format(language=language)
        )

    text = _normalise_special_chars(request.query)

    # Checked before acronym/synonym expansion: a bare "FD" should still
    # read as too short to act on, rather than being padded out by
    # expansion before the ambiguity check ever sees it.
    clarification = _check_needs_clarification(text)
    if clarification:
        logger.info("Query needs clarification: '%s'", request.query[:80])
        raise ClarificationNeededError(clarification)

    text = _expand_acronyms(text)
    text = _expand_synonyms(text)
    text = _add_implicit_domain_context(text)

    if text == request.query:
        return request
    logger.info("Query preprocessed: '%s' -> '%s'", request.query[:80], text[:80])
    return request.model_copy(update={"query": text})



####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\genai\rag\_reranker.py
####################################################################################################

"""
Cross-encoder reranker service for the YONO Business RAG pipeline.

Optional second-stage relevance reranking, applied to the candidate set
returned by :class:`~dlh_ai.genai.rag._retrieval.RetrievalService`'s vector
search before the top-k passages are handed to the LLM. A cross-encoder
scores each (query, passage) pair jointly, which is materially more
precise than embedding cosine similarity alone at the top of the ranking.

Fully optional and cheap when off:

* ``enabled=False`` (the ``[reranker].enabled`` config value) skips the
  HDFS download and model load entirely in :meth:`__post_init__` -
  :meth:`rerank` becomes a plain pass-through/truncate.
* When enabled, the model is loaded via ONNX Runtime (``optimum``) for
  faster CPU/GPU inference when available, falling back automatically to
  the torch ``CrossEncoder`` backend (``sentence-transformers``, already a
  hard dependency of this project) when ``optimum``/``onnxruntime`` are not
  installed or the ONNX load fails for any reason.
"""

import logging
import tempfile
import threading
from dataclasses import dataclass, field

import torch

from ...utils import HDFSClient
from ._models import SearchHit
from .exceptions import RerankerEncodingError, RerankerModelError

LOGGER = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")

ONNX_AVAILABLE = True
try:
    from optimum.onnxruntime import ORTModelForSequenceClassification
    from transformers import AutoTokenizer
    import onnxruntime  # noqa: F401
except ImportError:
    ONNX_AVAILABLE = False
    ORTModelForSequenceClassification = None
    AutoTokenizer = None

# Registry of known-good cross-encoder rerank models. This is documentation
# only, copy a "path" value into `[reranker].model_path` in config.toml to
# activate it - nothing in this module reads RERANK_MODELS at runtime.
RERANK_MODELS = [
    {
        "id": "ms_marco_minilm_l6_v2",
        "name": "cross-encoder/ms-marco-MiniLM-L-6-v2 (lightweight, English-only)",
        "path": "cross-encoder/ms-marco-MiniLM-L-6-v2",
    },
    {
        "id": "mxbai_rerank_large_v2",
        "name": "mixedbread-ai/mxbai-rerank-large-v2 (large, multilingual)",
        "path": "/dev/aiml/models/mixedbread-ai/mxbai-rerank-large-v2/",
    },
]


@dataclass
class RerankerService:
    """Optional cross-encoder reranker with an ONNX Runtime fast path.

    Parameters
    ----------
    enabled : bool
        When ``False``, no model is downloaded or loaded and
        :meth:`rerank` is a pass-through (only truncates to ``top_n``).
        Default is ``False``.
    model_path : str
        HDFS path (or a HuggingFace Hub id, e.g. when running locally with
        internet access) to the cross-encoder model. Required when
        ``enabled=True``.
    use_onnx : bool
        Attempt to load and run the model via ONNX Runtime (``optimum``)
        first. Falls back to the torch ``CrossEncoder`` backend if
        ``optimum``/``onnxruntime`` are not installed or the ONNX load
        fails. Default is ``True``.
    candidate_multiplier : int
        Retrieve ``top_k * candidate_multiplier`` candidates from vector
        search before reranking, then truncate back down to ``top_k``.
        Default is 4.
    max_length : int
        Max token length per (query, passage) pair. Default is 512.
    batch_size : int
        Pairs scored per forward pass. Default is 32.

    Raises
    ------
    RerankerModelError
        If ``enabled=True`` and the model cannot be loaded via either the
        ONNX or torch backend.

    Examples
    --------
    >>> reranker = RerankerService(enabled=False)
    >>> reranker.rerank("How do I block my card?", hits, top_n=5) == hits[:5]
    True
    """

    enabled: bool = False
    model_path: str = ""
    use_onnx: bool = True
    candidate_multiplier: int = 4
    max_length: int = 512
    batch_size: int = 32

    # Private, populated by __post_init__ only when enabled=True.
    device: str = field(init=False, default="cpu")
    _backend: str = field(init=False, default="disabled", repr=False)
    _model: object = field(init=False, default=None, repr=False)
    _tokenizer: object = field(init=False, default=None, repr=False)
    _cross_encoder: object = field(init=False, default=None, repr=False)
    _lock: threading.Lock = field(init=False, default_factory=threading.Lock, repr=False)

    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
        if not self.enabled:
            LOGGER.info(
                "Reranker disabled via config (reranker.enabled=false); "
                "skipping model load."
            )
            return

        if not self.model_path:
            raise RerankerModelError(
                "reranker.enabled=true but reranker.model_path is empty. "
                "Set 'reranker.model_path' in config.toml to a staged HDFS "
                "model directory."
            )

        self.device = self._resolve_device()
        self._load_model()

    # Public interface --------------------------------------------------

    def rerank(
        self,
        query: str,
        hits: list[SearchHit],
        top_n: int | None = None,
    ) -> list[SearchHit]:
        """Re-score and re-order candidate hits for a query.

        Parameters
        ----------
        query : str
            The user's natural-language question.
        hits : list[SearchHit]
            Candidate hits from a prior vector/hybrid search, in any order.
        top_n : int or None, optional
            Truncate the returned list to this many hits. ``None`` returns
            all of ``hits``, reordered. Default is ``None``.

        Returns
        -------
        list[SearchHit]
            When disabled: the first ``top_n`` of ``hits``, unchanged.
            When enabled: a new list of hits sorted by descending
            reranker score (each hit's ``score`` field replaced with the
            reranker's score), truncated to ``top_n``.

        Raises
        ------
        RerankerEncodingError
            If scoring the candidate pairs fails.
        """
        if not self.enabled or not hits:
            return hits[:top_n] if top_n is not None else hits

        pairs = [(query, f"{hit.question} {hit.answer}".strip()) for hit in hits]

        try:
            with self._lock:
                scores = self._score(pairs)
        except Exception as exc:
            raise RerankerEncodingError(
                f"Failed to rerank {len(hits)} candidate(s): "
                f"{type(exc).__name__} - {exc}"
            ) from exc

        scored_hits = sorted(
            zip(hits, scores), key=lambda pair: pair[1], reverse=True
        )
        if top_n is not None:
            scored_hits = scored_hits[:top_n]

        return [
            hit.model_copy(update={"score": float(score)})
            for hit, score in scored_hits
        ]

    # Private: device detection and model loading ------------------------

    @staticmethod
    def _resolve_device() -> str:
        """Return the best available torch device string."""
        if torch.cuda.is_available():
            device = "cuda:0"
            LOGGER.info("GPU detected: reranker model will run on %s.", device)
        else:
            device = "cpu"
            LOGGER.info("No CUDA GPU detected, reranker model will run on CPU.")
        return device

    def _load_model(self) -> None:
        """Download the model from HDFS and load it (ONNX first, torch fallback)."""
        hdfs_client = HDFSClient()

        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as temp_dir:
            local_dir = f"{temp_dir}/reranker_model"
            LOGGER.info("Downloading reranker model from HDFS: %s", self.model_path)
            hdfs_client.download(self.model_path, local_dir)
            LOGGER.debug("Reranker model downloaded to temporary path: %s", local_dir)

            if self.use_onnx and ONNX_AVAILABLE:
                try:
                    self._load_onnx(local_dir)
                    self._backend = "onnx"
                    LOGGER.info(
                        "Reranker loaded via ONNX Runtime (device=%s).", self.device
                    )
                    return
                except Exception as exc:
                    LOGGER.warning(
                        "ONNX reranker load failed (%s); falling back to the "
                        "torch CrossEncoder backend.",
                        exc,
                    )
            elif self.use_onnx and not ONNX_AVAILABLE:
                LOGGER.warning(
                    "reranker.use_onnx=true but 'optimum'/'onnxruntime' are not "
                    "installed; falling back to the torch CrossEncoder backend. "
                    "Install the 'onnx' extra to enable ONNX acceleration."
                )

            try:
                self._cross_encoder = self._load_cross_encoder(local_dir)
                self._backend = "torch"
                LOGGER.info(
                    "Reranker loaded via torch CrossEncoder (device=%s).", self.device
                )
            except Exception as exc:
                raise RerankerModelError(
                    f"Failed to load reranker model from '{self.model_path}' "
                    f"via either the ONNX or torch backend: "
                    f"{type(exc).__name__} - {exc}"
                ) from exc

    def _load_onnx(self, local_dir: str) -> None:
        """Load the model + tokenizer via optimum's ONNX Runtime backend."""
        provider = (
            "CUDAExecutionProvider"
            if self.device.startswith("cuda")
            else "CPUExecutionProvider"
        )
        self._tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(local_dir)
        try:
            self._model = ORTModelForSequenceClassification.from_pretrained(
                local_dir, provider=provider,
            )
        except Exception:
            # No pre-exported model.onnx in the snapshot; convert on the fly
            # from the local PyTorch checkpoint (no internet access needed).
            self._model = ORTModelForSequenceClassification.from_pretrained(
                local_dir, export=True, provider=provider,
            )

    def _load_cross_encoder(self, local_dir: str):
        """Load the model as a sentence-transformers torch CrossEncoder."""
        from sentence_transformers import CrossEncoder

        try:
            return CrossEncoder(
                local_dir, max_length=self.max_length, device=self.device
            )
        except ValueError as exc:
            if "trust_remote_code=True" in str(exc):
                LOGGER.warning(
                    "Reranker model requires trust_remote_code=True, "
                    "enabling automatically."
                )
                return CrossEncoder(
                    local_dir,
                    max_length=self.max_length,
                    device=self.device,
                    trust_remote_code=True,
                )
            raise

    # Private: scoring -----------------------------------------------------

    def _score(self, pairs: list[tuple[str, str]]) -> list[float]:
        """Dispatch batch scoring to whichever backend was loaded."""
        if self._backend == "onnx":
            return self._score_onnx(pairs)
        return list(
            self._cross_encoder.predict(
                pairs, batch_size=self.batch_size, show_progress_bar=False
            )
        )

    def _score_onnx(self, pairs: list[tuple[str, str]]) -> list[float]:
        """Score (query, passage) pairs via the ONNX Runtime model."""
        scores: list[float] = []
        for start in range(0, len(pairs), self.batch_size):
            batch = pairs[start : start + self.batch_size]
            queries = [pair[0] for pair in batch]
            passages = [pair[1] for pair in batch]

            inputs = self._tokenizer(
                queries,
                passages,
                padding=True,
                truncation=True,
                max_length=self.max_length,
                return_tensors="pt",
            )
            with torch.no_grad():
                logits = self._model(**inputs).logits.squeeze(-1)

            scores.extend(torch.sigmoid(logits).tolist())

        return scores



####################################################################################################
FILE: src\dlh_ai\genai\rag\_retrieval.py
####################################################################################################

"""
Vector search and retrieval service for the YONO Business RAG chatbot.

Provides a concurrency-safe ``RetrievalService`` class that handles:
* kNN vector search against Elasticsearch
* Hybrid search (kNN + BM25 combined via Elasticsearch multi-query)
* Result mapping from raw Elasticsearch hits to typed ``SearchHit`` objects
* LLM-powered answer synthesis via :class:`~dlh_ai.genai.rag._llm_service.LlmService`
* Input/output guardrail validation via :class:`~dlh_ai.genai.rag._guardrails.GuardrailService`

The LLM receives the top-k retrieved knowledge-base passages as context
(rendered through :class:`~dlh_ai.genai.prompt.PromptManager`) and
generates a coherent, concise answer for the user.

Designed to serve 10 million concurrent users via stateless instances
backed by an Elasticsearch cluster with appropriate replicas and shards.
"""

import logging
from dataclasses import dataclass, field

from elasticsearch import Elasticsearch

from dlh_ai.genai.prompt import PromptManager

from ._chat_history import ChatHistoryService, generate_session_id
from ._embedding_service import EmbeddingService
from ._guardrails import GuardrailService
from ._llm_service import LlmService
from ._models import ChatRequest, ChatResponse, SearchHit, VectorSearchResponse
from ._reranker import RerankerService
from .exceptions import ElasticsearchSearchError, LlmGenerationError, RetrievalError

LOGGER = logging.getLogger("dlh_ai")

# Elasticsearch source fields returned for every hit.
_SOURCE_FIELDS: list[str] = [
    "sno",
    "category",
    "question",
    "answer",
    "type",
    "combined_text",
    "document_id",
    "document_url",
    "ingestion_date",
    "title",
    "keywords",
    "sub_category",
]

# Prompt agent name: must match the YAML filename in the prompt directory.
_PROMPT_AGENT: str = "yono_business_chatbot"


@dataclass
class RetrievalService:
    """Stateless RAG service: vector retrieval + LLM answer synthesis.

    Combines kNN Elasticsearch search with the DLH Gemma-4 LLM to
    produce a synthesised, context-aware answer for each user query.

    All public methods are safe to call concurrently from multiple asyncio
    tasks or threads because they hold no mutable state between calls.
    All dependencies are injected at construction time.

    Parameters
    ----------
    client : Elasticsearch
        Active Elasticsearch client with a connection pool sized for the
        expected concurrency level.
    embedding_service : EmbeddingService
        Loaded and dimension-validated sentence-transformer embedding model.
    llm_service : LlmService
        Loaded DLH LLM client. Must have :meth:`~LlmService.load`
        called before any :meth:`answer_query` call.
    index_name : str
        Name of the Elasticsearch index that holds the knowledge base.
    prompt_manager : PromptManager
        Loaded prompt template manager used to render system and user prompts.
    guardrail_service : GuardrailService, optional
        When provided, all queries are run through input validation before
        retrieval and output validation before the response is returned.
        If ``None`` guardrails are skipped (useful for unit tests).
    reranker_service : RerankerService, optional
        When provided and enabled, the vector-search candidate pool is
        widened by ``reranker_service.candidate_multiplier`` and re-scored
        by a cross-encoder before being truncated back down to
        ``request.top_k``. If ``None`` or disabled, ranking is left as
        returned by :meth:`search`.
    chat_history_service : ChatHistoryService, optional
        When provided and enabled, prior turns for ``request.session_id``
        are loaded and fed into the ``rag_chat`` prompt as conversational
        memory, and the new turn is saved after the response is built. If
        ``None`` or disabled, every query is answered statelessly and no
        chat-history Elasticsearch calls are made. Regardless of this
        setting, a request with no ``session_id`` is always given a
        server-generated 12-digit one.
    source_table_url_template : str
        URL template (with a ``"{sno}"`` placeholder) for the "view source
        row" link included in every :class:`ChatResponse`. Empty string
        disables the link regardless of ``enable_source_table_link``.
    enable_source_table_link : bool
        Whether to compute and attach ``ChatResponse.source_table_link``.
        Default is False.

    Examples
    --------
    >>> service = RetrievalService(
    ...     client=es_client,
    ...     embedding_service=emb_service,
    ...     llm_service=llm_service,
    ...     index_name="yono_business_knowledge_base",
    ...     prompt_manager=PromptManager(),
    ... )
    >>> from dlh_ai.genai.rag._models import ChatRequest
    >>> req = ChatRequest(query="How do I block my debit card?", top_k=5)
    >>> response = service.answer_query(req)
    >>> print(response.answer)
    """

    client: Elasticsearch
    embedding_service: EmbeddingService
    llm_service: LlmService
    index_name: str
    prompt_manager: PromptManager = field(default_factory=PromptManager)
    guardrail_service: GuardrailService | None = field(default=None)
    reranker_service: RerankerService | None = field(default=None)
    chat_history_service: ChatHistoryService | None = field(default=None)
    source_table_url_template: str = field(default="")
    enable_source_table_link: bool = field(default=False)

    # Public search methods

    def search(self, query: str, top_k: int = 5) -> VectorSearchResponse:
        """Perform a kNN vector search and return ranked knowledge-base hits.

        The query is embedded using the same SentenceTransformer model used
        during ingestion so the query vector lives in the same semantic space
        as all document vectors.

        Parameters
        ----------
        query : str
            User's natural-language question.
        top_k : int, optional
            Maximum number of results to return. Must be >= 1. Default is 5.

        Returns
        -------
        VectorSearchResponse
            Typed response containing the query, total hit count, and a
            ranked list of :class:`SearchHit` objects.

        Raises
        ------
        ValueError
            Raised when ``query`` is empty or ``top_k`` < 1.
        ElasticsearchSearchError
            Raised when the Elasticsearch search call fails due to a network
            error or non-2xx HTTP response.
        RetrievalError
            Raised when the Elasticsearch response structure is unexpected.

        Notes
        -----
        ``num_candidates`` is set to ``max(top_k * 10, 50)`` per Elasticsearch
        kNN best-practice guidance for accuracy vs. latency trade-off.

        Examples
        --------
        >>> result = service.search("How do I transfer money via YONO?", top_k=5)
        >>> for hit in result.hits:
        ...     print(f"[{hit.score:.4f}] {hit.question}")
        """
        if not query or not query.strip():
            raise ValueError(
                "RetrievalService.search() received an empty query string. "
                "Provide a non-empty question."
            )
        if top_k < 1:
            raise ValueError(f"'top_k' must be at least 1, but received {top_k}.")

        LOGGER.debug("Encoding query for kNN search: '%s'", query[:100])
        query_vector: list[float] = self.embedding_service.encode_query(query)

        LOGGER.debug(
            "Executing kNN search on index '%s' (top_k=%d).", self.index_name, top_k
        )

        try:
            raw_response = self.client.search(
                index=self.index_name,
                knn={
                    "field": "combined_text_embedding",
                    "query_vector": query_vector,
                    "k": top_k,
                    "num_candidates": max(top_k * 10, 50),
                },
                source=_SOURCE_FIELDS,
            )
        except Exception as exc:
            raise ElasticsearchSearchError(
                f"kNN search on index '{self.index_name}' failed: "
                f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"
            ) from exc

        return self._parse_response(query=query, raw_response=raw_response)

    def search_hybrid(
        self,
        query: str,
        top_k: int = 5,
        bm25_boost: float = 0.3,
        vector_boost: float = 0.7,
    ) -> VectorSearchResponse:
        """Perform a hybrid kNN + BM25 search for improved recall.

        Combines dense vector similarity with sparse BM25 keyword matching.
        This improves recall for queries containing uncommon keywords not
        well-captured by semantic embeddings alone.

        Parameters
        ----------
        query : str
            User's natural-language question.
        top_k : int, optional
            Maximum number of results to return. Default is 5.
        bm25_boost : float, optional
            Boost factor for the BM25 ``multi_match`` sub-query. Default is 0.3.
        vector_boost : float, optional
            Boost factor for the kNN sub-query. Default is 0.7.

        Returns
        -------
        VectorSearchResponse
            Combined ranked results from both retrieval strategies.

        Raises
        ------
        ValueError
            Raised when ``query`` is empty or ``top_k`` < 1.
        ElasticsearchSearchError
            Raised when the Elasticsearch search call fails.
        RetrievalError
            Raised when the response structure is unexpected.

        Examples
        --------
        >>> result = service.search_hybrid("YONO UPI PIN reset", top_k=5)
        """
        if not query or not query.strip():
            raise ValueError(
